# Disco Dictionary: the glossary

Everything the in-game dictionary knows, readable without the game. Generated from `data/glossary/*.json` by `tools/generate_glossary_md.py`; edit those files, not this one.

Explanations are spoiler-free. Plot details are folded away under **Spoiler**, and a few names that would give the story away are collected in the last section.

## Skills

### Ancient Reptilian Brain

*Also: Reptilian Brain*

**A primal inner voice (not one of the 24 skills): deep, slow and comforting about oblivion.**

The oldest part of your brain. It speaks in the darkest moments, such as the very first lines of the game and when you are close to death, and it tempts you to give up and sink back into nothing.

See also: [Limbic System](#limbic-system), [Spinal Cord](#spinal-cord)

### Authority

**Psyche skill: asserting yourself and commanding respect.**

Authority is the voice that demands obedience and gets offended when you're disrespected. It helps you intimidate and take control of a conversation, and it tends to push you toward shouting.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Half Light](#half-light), [Physical Instrument](#physical-instrument)

### Composure

**Motorics skill: poker face, body language and keeping it together in public.**

Composure controls how you hold yourself: not flinching, not crying, reading other people's body language. It also has strong opinions about clothing. Very high Composure can make it hard to show any emotion at all.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Drama](#drama), [Savoir Faire](#savoir-faire)

### Conceptualization

**Intellect skill: creativity and art. Sees artistic meaning and comes up with original ideas.**

Conceptualization judges art, invents metaphors and proposes creative solutions. It is often grandiose and harshly critical of anything it considers unoriginal.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire)

### Drama

**Intellect skill: acting and lying, and telling when others are lying.**

Drama is your inner actor. It speaks in a theatrical, flowery way. It helps you bluff and warns you when someone is putting on a performance, but it can also make you paranoid and melodramatic.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Empathy](#empathy), [Composure](#composure)

### Electrochemistry

**Physique skill: appetites. Drugs, alcohol, sex, and knowing how to party.**

Electrochemistry speaks for your brain chemistry and cravings. It knows everything about substances and pleasure, encourages you to indulge, and is very persuasive about it. It is a big part of why the detective is in the state he is in.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Volition](#volition)

### Empathy

**Psyche skill: understanding how other people feel.**

Empathy reads emotions behind words and faces: who is grieving, who is ashamed, who is hiding pain. It makes you kinder, and also more easily hurt.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Drama](#drama), [Suggestion](#suggestion)

### Encyclopedia

**Intellect skill: your store of trivia about history, science and culture.**

Encyclopedia is the voice that explains the world: who a historical figure was, what a political movement believed, what a building used to be. It is one of the best sources of lore in the game, but it often recalls useless facts while forgetting important personal ones (like your own name).

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Logic](#logic), [Rhetoric](#rhetoric)

### Endurance

**Physique skill: stamina and toughness. Sets how much Health you have.**

Endurance keeps you going on no sleep and a ruined body. Your maximum Health depends on it.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Health](#health), [Pain Threshold](#pain-threshold)

### Esprit de Corps

**Psyche skill: cop culture and loyalty to your fellow officers. Shows you glimpses of them elsewhere.**

Esprit de Corps understands what it means to be police. Its special trick is the 'flash-sideways': short scenes showing what other officers are doing or thinking at that moment, far away from you.

*Real-world inspiration:* French for 'spirit of the body (corps)': the morale and loyalty that bind members of a group, especially soldiers or police.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Half Light

**Physique skill: fight-or-flight. Fear, aggression and sensing danger.**

Half Light is the animal alarm system. It warns you of threats, makes you jumpy and suspicious, and urges you to strike first. It is useful in danger and dangerous in conversation.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Authority](#authority), [Reaction Speed](#reaction-speed)

### Hand/Eye Coordination

*Also: Hand-Eye Coordination, Hand Eye Coordination*

**Motorics skill: aim and precision. Shooting, throwing and steady hands.**

Hand/Eye Coordination is about accuracy: firing a gun, throwing something, doing delicate work. It is fond of guns.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Reaction Speed](#reaction-speed)

### Inland Empire

**Psyche skill: imagination, hunches and the uncanny. Lets you 'hear' objects and dreams.**

Inland Empire is the mystical, dreamlike part of your mind. It gives inanimate things voices (your necktie, even a corpse), offers gut feelings that turn out to be true, and drifts into the surreal. It is often right in ways that make no logical sense.

*Real-world inspiration:* Almost certainly named after David Lynch's 2006 film 'Inland Empire', known for its dream logic. (The Inland Empire is also a real region of California.)

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Horrific Necktie](#horrific-necktie), [Shivers](#shivers), [Conceptualization](#conceptualization)

### Intellect

**Attribute: raw brain power. Governs Logic, Encyclopedia, Rhetoric, Drama, Conceptualization and Visual Calculus.**

One of your four attributes, described in the game as your capacity to reason. Your Intellect score sets the starting level and the maximum level of its six skills. High-Intellect characters spot contradictions, remember trivia, win arguments and read art.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Physique](#physique), [Motorics](#motorics), [Skill check](#skill-check)

### Interfacing

**Motorics skill: machines and mechanisms. Locks, radios, radiocomputers.**

Interfacing understands how things work with your hands: picking locks, fixing devices, operating radiocomputers. It also gives you an almost tactile sense of machinery.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Radiocomputer](#radiocomputer)

### Limbic System

**A primal inner voice (not one of the 24 skills): the raw, hurting emotional core of your mind.**

The Limbic System speaks for old pain and fear. It appears at the start of the game alongside the Ancient Reptilian Brain, and in moments of emotional crisis. It is cruel because it knows exactly what hurts.

See also: [Ancient Reptilian Brain](#ancient-reptilian-brain), [Spinal Cord](#spinal-cord)

### Logic

*Also: Puzzle Face*

**Intellect skill: reasoning and deduction. Spots contradictions and connects facts.**

Logic is the cold, analytical voice. It points out when a story doesn't add up and pieces evidence together. At high levels it makes you so proud of your own brilliance that intellectual flattery works on you, and you can miss clues.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Visual Calculus](#visual-calculus), [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia)

### Motorics

**Attribute: coordination and senses. Governs Hand/Eye Coordination, Perception, Reaction Speed, Savoir Faire, Interfacing and Composure.**

One of your four attributes. Reflexes, fine motor control, noticing details, style and keeping a straight face.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Psyche](#psyche), [Physique](#physique)

### Pain Threshold

**Physique skill: withstanding pain, physical and emotional.**

Pain Threshold helps you shrug off injuries and keep going. It has a darker side: it can come to enjoy pain and push you toward things that hurt.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Endurance](#endurance), [Health](#health)

### Perception

**Motorics skill: noticing things with your senses: sight, hearing, smell and taste.**

Perception catches the small details others miss: a smell in the air, something half-hidden, a sound in the distance. It is one of the most frequently heard voices.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Visual Calculus](#visual-calculus)

### Physical Instrument

**Physique skill: raw strength and physical presence.**

Physical Instrument is your inner gym coach: loud, blunt and convinced every problem can be solved with muscles. It helps with lifting, breaking and intimidating.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Authority](#authority), [Half Light](#half-light)

### Physique

**Attribute: the body. Governs Endurance, Pain Threshold, Physical Instrument, Electrochemistry, Shivers and Half Light.**

One of your four attributes. Strength, toughness and appetites. Your Health (your physical health bar) comes from this side of you.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Psyche](#psyche), [Motorics](#motorics), [Health](#health)

### Psyche

**Attribute: emotional intelligence and willpower. Governs Volition, Inland Empire, Empathy, Authority, Esprit de Corps and Suggestion.**

One of your four attributes. It covers reading people, influencing them and keeping yourself together. Your Morale (your mental health bar) comes from this side of you.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Physique](#physique), [Motorics](#motorics), [Morale](#morale)

### Reaction Speed

**Motorics skill: reflexes and quick wits.**

Reaction Speed lets you dodge, catch and answer fast. It also notices quick changes, like someone's expression flickering.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Half Light](#half-light)

### Rhetoric

**Intellect skill: argument, debate and politics. Picks apart what people say.**

Rhetoric helps you win debates, notice loaded words and recognise political positions. It loves ideology, and it can make you rigid and argumentative.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Drama](#drama), [Communism](#communism), [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Moralism](#moralism), [Fascism](#fascism)

### Savoir Faire

**Motorics skill: style and agility. Jumping, sneaking, stealing, and looking cool doing it.**

Savoir Faire wants you to be effortlessly cool. It handles acrobatics, sleight of hand and flair, and it is crushed when you embarrass yourself.

*Real-world inspiration:* French for 'knowing how to do': social grace, poise and confidence.

See also: [Motorics](#motorics), [Composure](#composure)

### Shivers

**Physique skill: a supernatural connection to the city. Lets you sense what is happening elsewhere.**

Shivers lets the city of Revachol speak to you. When it triggers, you feel the wind and the cold and suddenly perceive scenes somewhere else in the district. Its voice is often written as if the city itself is talking.

See also: [Physique](#physique), [Revachol](#revachol), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire)

### Spinal Cord

**A primal inner voice (not one of the 24 skills): pure, thoughtless physical action.**

The Spinal Cord speaks rarely, when the body takes over completely and thinking stops.

See also: [Ancient Reptilian Brain](#ancient-reptilian-brain), [Limbic System](#limbic-system)

### Suggestion

**Psyche skill: charm and soft manipulation. Getting people to do what you want.**

Suggestion is the smooth talker. Where Authority pushes, Suggestion persuades: it knows what to say to make someone like you or agree with you.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Empathy](#empathy), [Authority](#authority)

### Visual Calculus

**Intellect skill: spatial reasoning. Reconstructs crime scenes, angles and trajectories.**

Visual Calculus turns a scene into geometry: where a shot came from, how a body fell, what distance something was thrown. It is the forensic, almost mathematical part of your detective work.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Logic](#logic), [Perception](#perception)

### Volition

**Psyche skill: willpower and self-respect. Keeps your Morale up and resists temptation.**

Volition is the steady voice that tells you to pull yourself together, stop drinking and treat yourself (and others) decently. It also protects your Morale. If the other voices are a crowd, Volition is the one trying to keep order.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Morale](#morale), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

## Thoughts

### Ace's High

**Revachol's name for a high five, a custom started by the aerostatic brigades of the Revolution; slapping hands with Kim gives you a Thought of that name.**

Revacholians use it to celebrate success, especially in sports, and the gesture has spread around the world even though the revolutionaries who invented it lost. Kim says his precinct, the 57th, kept the custom alive, and leaving him hanging does not go down well.

You can follow it up with an Ace's Low.

See also: [Ace's Low](#aces-low), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Revolution](#revolution), [Aerostatic](#aerostatic), [Precinct 57](#precinct-57)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Kim first offers one after you get the body down from the tree behind the Whirling-in-Rags. With the Thought internalized, you can ask Kim whether his jacket is from the revolutionary air brigades.

</details>

### Ace's Low

**The follow-up to an Ace's High: you turn your back after the high five and wait for a second, low slap; doing it with Kim gives you a Thought.**

Interfacing suggests it as an optional flourish. Kim, ever precise, does not leave an Ace's Low hanging.

See also: [Ace's High](#aces-high), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Art Cop

**A Thought and copotype for a detective who judges everything as art: half art critic, half cop.**

Conceptualization offers it once you have made enough artsy or critical remarks about paintings, music, books and buildings. It urges you to hunt down not just street criminals but trite, derivative artists and writers too. Show off your taste and people like Kim and Cindy may start calling you Art Cop.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Conceptualization](#conceptualization), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet)

### Boring Cop

*Also: Regular Cop*

**A copotype Thought Drama offers if you keep choosing the plain, by-the-book answers; Drama announces that your copotype is 'Regular Cop'.**

Picking dull, safe options throughout the game quietly builds up this copotype. Eventually Drama teases you for always taking 'the regular, please, the brown', and offers to make it official. You can opt in, which adds the Boring Cop thought to your Thought Cabinet, or opt out.

Elsewhere, when Suggestion asks you to guess your copotype, you can suggest Regular Cop; Suggestion insists you are the Sorry Cop instead.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Drama](#drama), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet)

### Coach Physical Instrument

**A Thought in which your Physical Instrument appoints itself your drill-sergeant gym coach, determined to whip your neglected body back into shape.**

Physical Instrument eventually insists on coaching you, promising push-ups, blood, sweat, piss and tears until you become a Master Athlete; you can opt in or turn it down. With the coach on board it barks motivational abuse at you in all sorts of situations, from your choice of trousers to climbing and throwing, and even Authority thinks it overdoes things.

See also: [Physical Instrument](#physical-instrument), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Thought](#thought)

### Hobocop

*Also: hobo cop*

**A Thought about embracing life as a homeless cop: sleeping rough and digging through bins instead of paying for a room.**

You can get it early on: after Garte asks whether you want to become a hobo, your mind wonders where home is, and you can answer that you'll live in a dumpster. Your fellow officers also joke about 'going full hobocop' as if it were an urban myth.

See also: [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Garte](#garte), [Copotype](#copotype)

### Kingdom of Conscience

**Empathy's name for the far-off moralist ideal; it invites you to join once your political answers keep landing in the sensible middle.**

Empathy notices your 'normal, reasonable, temperate' opinions and offers you citizenship. The Kingdom, it says, is not a place but a rare moment in history that comes about only gradually: post-capitalist, post-national, post-ideological, and perhaps not reached even in your grandchildren's lifetime. It credits Dolores Dei with seeing that progress is worthless without stability.

You can question it or opt out; opting in adds a moralist thought to your Thought Cabinet.

See also: [Moralism](#moralism), [Moralintern](#moralintern), [Centrism](#centrism), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Empathy](#empathy)

### Remote Viewer

*Also: Remote Viewer Du Bois*

**A Thought you can be offered after repeatedly claiming psychic insight: Inland Empire 'inducts' you into the Remote Viewers Division.**

Every time you explain things by extrasensory perception, ghosts or 'the veil', the game keeps count. After a few such claims, Inland Empire asks whether you feel something para-natural, then has you close your eyes and tune in to 'RVD HQ' while Kim looks on, unimpressed. You can discard the thought at several points, or let it connect and add Remote Viewer to your Thought Cabinet.

With it, extra para-natural dialogue options open up around Martinaise.

*Real-world inspiration:* Remote viewing, the supposed psychic ability to see distant places, once studied by US military and intelligence programmes.

See also: [Remote Viewers Division](#remote-viewers-division), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet)

### Sorry Cop

**A Thought offered when you keep apologising and running yourself down: Suggestion announces that your copotype is the Sorry Cop.**

The game quietly counts how often you apologise or call yourself a failure. Once you've done it enough, Suggestion makes your copotype 'official' and offers you the thought.

You can also introduce yourself as the Sorry Cop in conversation.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Suggestion](#suggestion), [Superstar Cop](#superstar-cop), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet)

### Superstar Cop

**A Thought offered when you keep bragging about yourself: Composure declares you the world's first celebrity police officer.**

The game counts your boastful, attention-seeking choices. Once there are enough, Composure confirms that you're a Superstar Cop and invites you to shout 'action' and reinvent your messy life as a star vehicle.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Composure](#composure), [Sorry Cop](#sorry-cop), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet)

## Game Mechanics

### Archetype

*Also: archetypes*

**A ready-made starting character (Thinker, Sensitive or Physical) offered at the start.**

The archetypes are preset spreads of attribute points: the Thinker leans on Intellect, the Sensitive on Psyche, the Physical on Physique. You can also build your own detective.

See also: [Intellect](#intellect), [Psyche](#psyche), [Physique](#physique), [Signature skill](#signature-skill)

### Check difficulty

*Also: Trivial, Challenging, Formidable, Legendary, Heroic, Godly*

**The named difficulty levels on skill checks, from Trivial up to Impossible.**

From easiest to hardest: Trivial (6), Easy (8), Medium (10), Challenging (12), Formidable (13), Legendary (14), Heroic (15), Godly (16), Impossible (18 and up). The number is the total you need on two dice plus your skill and bonuses.

See also: [Skill check](#skill-check)

### Copotype

*Also: copotypes*

**The kind of cop your choices make you, e.g. Superstar Cop, Sorry Cop, Apocalypse Cop, Boring Cop.**

Like your politics, your style of policing is tracked: grandiose and showy (Superstar Cop), forever apologising (Sorry Cop), obsessed with the end of the world (Apocalypse Cop), sensible and by-the-book (Boring Cop), and others. Each comes with its own reactions and Thoughts.

See also: [Political alignment](#political-alignment)

### Health

**Your physical health bar. If it hits zero, you die and the game ends.**

Health is lost through injuries and physical strain and comes from Endurance. You can restore it with healing items. Keep an eye on both Health and Morale: losing either one ends the game.

See also: [Morale](#morale), [Endurance](#endurance), [Physique](#physique)

### Internalize

*Also: internalizing, internalized, internalise, internalising, internalised*

**To research a Thought in your Thought Cabinet until it becomes part of you.**

See also: [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Thought](#thought)

### Morale

**Your mental health bar. If it hits zero, you give up on being a cop and the game ends.**

Morale is lost through humiliation, despair and emotional blows. It comes from Volition. Like Health, it can be restored with certain items.

See also: [Health](#health), [Volition](#volition), [Psyche](#psyche)

### Orb

*Also: orbs*

**The small white bubbles that appear over things in the world. Click one to hear a skill's comment.**

Orbs are passive observations tied to a place or object. Walking near something can trigger one; clicking it plays a short line from one of your skills. Hold Tab to highlight everything you can interact with nearby.

See also: [Passive check](#passive-check)

### Passive check

*Also: passive checks*

**An automatic check you never see rolled. It's why skills suddenly interrupt with comments.**

Passive checks compare your skill level to a hidden difficulty without rolling dice. If your skill is high enough, that skill chimes in during conversation with an observation or idea. Higher skills therefore mean that voice speaks more often.

See also: [Skill check](#skill-check)

### Political alignment

*Also: political alignments, political vision*

**The game tracks which political direction your answers lean: communist, ultraliberal, moralist or fascist.**

Many dialogue options are tagged with a political leaning. Picking them shifts your detective's politics, unlocks related Thoughts, and in The Final Cut can open political 'vision quests'. None of them is required, and the game satirises all four.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Moralism](#moralism), [Fascism](#fascism), [Copotype](#copotype)

### Red check

*Also: red checks*

**A one-time skill check. If you fail it, you can never retry it.**

Red checks are shown in red. They are moments that happen once, so the result sticks. Failing is not a game over: failures often lead to interesting (or funny) outcomes, and the story continues.

See also: [Skill check](#skill-check), [White check](#white-check)

### Signature skill

**The one skill you picked at character creation. It gets a bonus and a higher maximum.**

See also: [Skill point](#skill-point), [Archetype](#archetype)

### Skill check

*Also: skill checks*

**A dice roll: two six-sided dice plus your skill level, against a difficulty number.**

When you pick a dialogue option marked like [Logic - Medium 10], the game rolls two dice (2d6), adds your Logic and any bonuses, and you succeed if the total reaches the target. Rolling double sixes always succeeds, and double ones always fails. Hover over a check before choosing it to see your exact chance.

See also: [White check](#white-check), [Red check](#red-check), [Passive check](#passive-check), [Check difficulty](#check-difficulty)

### Skill point

*Also: skill points*

**Earned each time you level up. Spend it to raise a skill or unlock a Thought Cabinet slot.**

You gain experience (XP) for progressing the story and passing checks, and each level up gives you a skill point. Each skill has a maximum (its cap) that depends on its attribute and on whether it is your signature skill. Raising a skill also unlocks failed white checks for it.

See also: [White check](#white-check), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Signature skill](#signature-skill)

### Suicide Cop

**A grim copotype: if you keep making suicidal remarks, your skills start calling you the Suicide Cop.**

The game quietly counts suicidal remarks, such as threatening to shoot yourself or saying you should have killed yourself. Once enough add up, Pain Threshold names you the Suicide Cop while other skills argue for and against it, and a related dark Thought can become available.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Pain Threshold](#pain-threshold), [Volition](#volition)

### Thought

*Also: thoughts*

**An idea you can place in the Thought Cabinet to research.**

Thoughts are unlocked by things you say and do. Each one has a research time, an effect while you are thinking about it, and a different effect once it is complete.

See also: [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Internalize](#internalize)

### Thought Cabinet

**A menu of ideas you can 'internalize': think about over time to gain permanent effects.**

During the game you discover Thoughts: ideas, obsessions, ideologies. Place one in a free slot of the Thought Cabinet and it is researched over in-game time (usually with a temporary effect). When it finishes, you get its final, permanent effect. Some are useful, some are jokes, some change how characters treat you. You can unlock more slots with skill points, and forget a thought later if you need the slot.

See also: [Thought](#thought), [Internalize](#internalize), [Skill point](#skill-point)

### White check

*Also: white checks*

**A skill check you can retry later if you fail, after improving the skill involved.**

White checks are shown in white. If one fails, it locks until you put a point into that skill (or something relevant changes), then you can try again. They are safe to attempt.

See also: [Skill check](#skill-check), [Red check](#red-check), [Skill point](#skill-point)

## People

### Acele

*Also: Acele Berger*

**A young sound experimenter kneeling on the frozen bay with a contact microphone; she belongs to a crew of speedfreaks who want to open a club in the old church.**

Acele Berger is usually the first of the group you meet, out on the ice near the church, listening to sounds under the water and ignoring the cold (and you). She is blunt and distracted, but she can tell you what she has seen in the church and what her friends are planning.

Her crew is Andre, Noid and Egg Head. According to Evrart Claire, she is the daughter of a local tough known as Mico the Kebab.

See also: [Andre](#andre), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire)

### Alain

**A tattooed Hardie Boy who acts as Titus Hardie's right-hand man; openly hostile to cops and quick to reach for his holster.**

You meet him with the rest of the Hardie Boys at their table in the Whirling-in-Rags. He looks to be in his late thirties or early forties, possibly Mesque, and he is the least nervous of the gang when questioned, as if he has been through it many times before.

See also: [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He survives the Tribunal shootout. Depending on how it ends, you may later find him back at the Whirling, drunk and broken.

</details>

### Alice

*Also: Alice DeMettrie, Alice de Mettrie, Officer Alice DeMettrie*

**The radio officer at Precinct 57 who answers when you use Kim's motor carriage radio; she can patch calls through and run checks for you.**

Alice handles dispatch for Kim's station. Through her you can report findings, have serial numbers checked against databases and get connected to outside numbers. She is noticeably friendlier with the lieutenant than with you.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Kineema](#kineema), [Precinct 57](#precinct-57)

### Andre

**The fast-talking leader of a young speedfreak crew camped on the ice by the old church, which they want to turn into a dance club.**

Andre bleaches the tips of his hair with a toothbrush and calls his group (Noid, Egg Head and Acele) 'music venue organizers'. He wants the police to deal with the 'spooky' people holed up in the church, so his crew can open the wildest club in Revachol there for their anodic dance music.

See also: [Acele](#acele), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Their original plan to fund the club involved a speed lab inside the church; you can uncover it and talk them out of it.

</details>

### Annette

**A cheerful little girl minding the book stands outside her mother's bookstore in the cold; she is a big fan of the detective Dick Mullen.**

Her mother Plaisance owns the shop, called 'Crime, Romance, and Biographies of Famous People', which also sells postcards and board games. Annette is friendly and a little cheeky, and at first she finds you a poor match for the fictional Dick Mullen.

She has also told her mother that their shop is cursed.

See also: [Dick Mullen](#dick-mullen), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If you do some good police work around town, she may later give you a hat just like the one Dick Mullen wears.

</details>

### Barry the Butcher

**A butcher stranded on the far side of the broken canal water lock, impatient for the repairs so he can get his meat to sell.**

You can see him waving from across the canal, in a yellow shirt and grey overalls. His friend on your side keeps him company by eating Barry's salami. He is not happy about the smashed billboard blocking the water lock, or with whoever knocked it down.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise), [Coupris 40](#coupris-40)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The billboard was knocked into the canal by a speeding motor carriage, and it is one you know very well.

</details>

### Billie Méjean

*Also: Billie, Méjeans*

**The name on a soggy Jamrock library card found on a dead man on the Martinaise boardwalk; tracing it is how you find out who he was.**

The card was issued by the Central Jamrock Public Library. Calling the library from Kim's motor carriage turns up a home address in Martinaise: apartment 20 of the Capeside Apartments.

See also: [Capeside Apartments](#capeside-apartments), [Jamrock](#jamrock)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Billie is a woman: the dead man's wife. Her husband, Victor Méjean, had been using her card. She lives with Victor and their two daughters, Jennie and Jolie, and she is also the 'working class woman' you can meet browsing the book stand on the plaza, insisting her husband isn't missing. You and Kim have to deliver the news of his death.

</details>

### Bird's Nest Roy

*Also: Roy, Roy's Nest*

**The soft-spoken pawnbroker by the Martinaise canal; 'Bird's Nest Roy' is both his nickname and what people call his shop.**

You can pawn items here for cash, and the shop sells odds and ends such as boomboxes. Roy is enchanted by light shows and sound: long ago he was part of a group of young musicians who gave up music to record unusual noises, producing the 'Simfonia Col Do Ma Ma Daqua'.

He served in the Emergency Relief Brigade after the People's Pile disaster (a failed generator that leaked radioactive waste) and has taken Pyrholidon for radiation sickness ever since.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

During your binge you pawned your own service pistol to Roy, and he has already sold it on to someone else.

</details>

### Call Me Mañana

*Also: Mañana*

**A jolly, laid-back union dockworker at the harbour gates whose name advertises his dislike of work; he can fill you in on the strike.**

Mañana is the first person you meet at the bottom of the harbour gates. He explains the strike and the 'scabs' trying to take the dockworkers' jobs, talks about the union boss, and has tips for dealing with Measurehead. Though the name sounds Mesque, he calls himself a 'Vacholiero' and lives by the ideals of the boiadeiro, a free cow herder of the northern Mesque steppes.

Evrart Claire calls him the union's free agent for 'special operations' and may send you to him for a key. He is also friends with the Whirling's cook.

See also: [Union](#union), [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Strike](#strike), [Scab](#scab), [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Catelyn Mijanou

*Also: Mijanou, professor Mijanou*

**A renowned geologist who, about 70 years ago, discovered the Cryobacter katlensis, a microscopic cryptid frozen in ancient ice.**

Lena tells the story: the bacteria had survived in the ice of Katla's Boreal Plateau since before recorded history. Mijanou injected herself with them, stopped ageing, grew eccentric and irritable, spoke a lot about the end of the world, and then vanished. Morell counts her among the few academics he respects.

See also: [Lena](#lena), [Cryptid](#cryptid), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology)

### Chad Tillbrook

*Also: Tillbrook, Patrol Officer Tillbrook*

**A junior patrol officer at Precinct 41 whom Esprit de Corps keeps showing you, going about honest everyday police work in Jamrock.**

In the visions he types up reports in the station's converted silk mill, refuses a bribe from a local hood, puts down a rabid dog in the Valley of Dogs, and listens patiently while a young man on Boogie Street asks whether his warts are cancer. In one of them he talks with his fellow officer Émile Mollins.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps), [Boogie Street](#boogie-street)

### Chester McLaine

*Also: Satellite-Officer McLaine, McLaine*

**A tall, lean, ginger-haired officer of Precinct 41 and Mack Torson's partner; he has a famous story about faking his own death.**

McLaine joins in the mockery when you radio the station, cracking jokes about your missing gun, badge and dignity. In Esprit de Corps visions he is often seen with Torson, telling the tale of tailing a man called 'Francis the Shoe'.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Mack Torson](#mack-torson), [Jules Pidieu](#jules-pidieu), [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps)

### Cindy the SKULL

*Also: Cindy*

**A sharp-tongued teenage graffiti artist from the SKULLS gang, waiting to paint a giant mural by the pier; she calls you 'pig' and refuses to snitch.**

Cindy is waiting for the right words for her mural and spends her time glaring at Joyce Messier on the boat nearby, which makes her a natural introduction to Joyce. She leans communist and has nothing but contempt for the police, though she can be coaxed into sharing some gossip about crime in Martinaise.

The SKULLS are a Revachol West street gang that seems half art movement; Cindy is fond of the kids who want to join. She also owns a paintbrush, handy if you want to paint a wall yourself.

See also: [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Pig](#pig), [Communism](#communism)

### Claire brothers

*Also: Claires, the Claires, brothers Claire*

**Evrart Claire and his twin brother Edgar, who together run the dockworkers' Union and, many say, all of Martinaise.**

Joyce Messier says the twins look and talk exactly alike (Edgar has a lazy eye), and that when one brother's term as foreman ends the other takes over. She calls them the most corrupt and nepotistic men she has dealt with, and suspects they had earlier union leaders assassinated. Kim believes nothing happens in Martinaise without the Claires knowing.

They grew up in Martinaise; Gaston Martin taught them history and human studies at school, alongside Easy Leo.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Edgar Claire](#edgar-claire), [Union](#union), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

### Cleaning Lady

**A coughing old woman who cleans the hallways of the apartment building by the pier; she knows every tenant and has opinions about all of them.**

She lets you into the building and lives in a tiny room upstairs next to the coal room. Ask her where someone lives and she will usually point the way, with a sour remark about the neighbours. She also asks you to look into an apartment that is supposed to be empty but clearly is not.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise), [Cuno's Dad](#cunos-dad)

### Cuno

*Also: Cunn, Kuuno, Kuuno de Ruyter*

**A foul-mouthed kid who throws rocks at the hanging body and talks about himself in the third person.**

Cuno is a kid: aggressive and constantly swearing ('Cuno doesn't care'). He hangs around near the Whirling with his friend Cunoesse. Underneath the bluster he is a neglected child.

See also: [Cunoesse](#cunoesse), [Pig](#pig)

### Cuno's Dad

*Also: Uuno de Ruyter*

**Cuno's father, Uuno de Ruyter, whom Cuno brags is the most violent man in Revachol; he lives in the apartment building by the pier.**

To hear Cuno tell it, his dad is a raging monster on steroids and speed who does not care about anything. His apartment, number 12, has a chained door with an unpaid electricity bill pinned to it, and the cleaning lady would rather not talk about the family.

See also: [Cleaning Lady](#cleaning-lady), [Cuno](#cuno)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Inside you find not a monster but an ageing ginger alcoholic lying in a stupor on his bed, barely able to mumble insults at you.

</details>

### Cunoesse

**Cuno's friend and partner in crime: just as foul-mouthed.**

See also: [Cuno](#cuno)

### De Paule

*Also: Phillis de Paule*

**Phillis de Paule, one of the mercenaries in Martinaise and their radio operator; Joyce Messier can tell you about her.**

She is one of the hired soldiers looking after the company's side of the strike, and she keeps watch from somewhere out of sight, possibly one of the buildings overlooking the roundabout.

See also: [Mercenaries](#mercenaries), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Wild Pines](#wild-pines)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She turns up armoured at the Tribunal, cold and eager to kill, insisting she heard the Hardie Boys boast about killing her commander. She dies in the shootout.

</details>

### Don't Call Abigail

**A very drunk man in the fishing village who can only mumble about not calling Abigail; one of the self-styled Union of Moribund Alcoholics.**

He is unshaven, red-nosed and barely conscious, and whatever you ask, the answer comes back to Abigail, most likely a wife or girlfriend who is long gone. His drinking buddy Idiot Doom Spiral does most of the talking for the group.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He seems to recognise you, because you drank with this crowd during your binge.

</details>

### Drysant

**King Frissel's young cousin, a vain royal officer whose disastrous attack early in the Revolution nearly got René Arnoux killed.**

René still despises him. Drysant went into battle in purple velvet and cockatoo feathers with a gold-plated rifle, and marched his men single-file into partisan-held Couron while riding ahead on a red stallion. The rebels let them come close, then opened fire.

Both men survived: René crawled for two days with the badly wounded, jawless Drysant until cavalry found them, and Drysant had the king give René a medal for it.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Croix de Bravoure](#croix-de-bravoure), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revolution](#revolution)

### Easy Leo

*Also: Leonard Bellec, Leo*

**Leonard Bellec, a small, cheerful, endlessly talkative Union worker who repaints container covers in the harbour yard; he loves Evrart and gossip.**

Leo works in the container yard of the harbour near Evrart Claire's office, singing little songs to himself as he swaps the Wild Pines container covers for red Union ones. He is friendly, childlike and loyal, and he will talk about anyone and anything if you let him, often wandering off into stories about his school days.

He went to school with Evrart and his brother Edgar, and Gaston Martin was once his teacher. He admits his memory is poor and he leaves notes for himself. Because he says more than Evrart would like, he is a handy source of local information, for example about the Union's lawyer Elizabeth.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Union](#union), [Harbour](#harbour), [Elizabeth](#elizabeth), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

### Echo Maker

**The last member of Steban's underground communist reading group; he repeats and amplifies whatever Steban says, much to Steban's irritation.**

You find him with Steban, the Student Communist, in a disused part of the Capeside Apartments, where the two run a reading group that has expelled almost everyone else for lacking ideological fortitude. He is a hanger-on and a toady: he echoes Steban's opinions, adds pompous theory, recommends difficult books and gossips about former comrades.

The pair also play the fantasy game Wirrâl together, and he has strong opinions about it.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov)

### Edgar Claire

*Also: Edgar, Mr. Edgar*

**Evrart Claire's twin brother and fellow boss of the dockworkers' Union; he is away from Revachol on union business while you are in Martinaise.**

People say he looks and talks just like Evrart, apart from a lazy eye, and the twins take turns as the Union's foreman. Easy Leo calls him a very smart fellow, and Joyce Messier rates him as even more venal and nepotistic than his brother.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Claire brothers](#claire-brothers), [Union](#union)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The old deserter on the island reveals that Edgar sought him out about twenty years ago, around the time the Claires came to power. Edgar studied at a university in East Revachol and can talk about the class struggle and quote Mazov. The deserter hints that the union leader of that time, Tiphaine Holly, who supposedly ran away, was in fact shot, but he refuses to testify against Edgar.

</details>

### Egg Head

*Also: Germaine, Germaine Egg Head, Germaine Egg-Head, Germaine van der Wijk*

**A peroxide-blond, wildly enthusiastic party boy in a group of young ravers camped by the old church; he shouts 'HARD CORE!' at everything.**

Egg Head is one of four young people (with Andre, Noid and Acele) camped on the ice next to the abandoned church, which they dream of turning into a club for 'anodic music', their brand of electronic dance music. He carries a tape player, wears big boiadeiro boots and fires off nonsense rhymes and slogans at full volume.

He calls himself 'just the party boy': he keeps the mood up while the others handle the technical work. Under the bravado he is sweet, naive and worried that their club will never happen.

*Real-world inspiration:* H.P. Baxxter, frontman of the German techno group Scooter; Egg Head's shouts echo Scooter lyrics such as 'How much is the fish?'.

See also: [boiadeiro](#boiadeiro), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

His papers give his full name as Germaine van der Wijk.

</details>

### Elizabeth

*Also: Elizabeth Beaufort, Miss Beaufort, Lizzy, Lizzie, Liz, Miss Beaufort, Lizzy, Elizabeth, Lizzy Beaufort*

**Elizabeth Beaufort, the sharp, cold legal counsellor of the Dockworkers' Union; she sits in on your questioning of the Hardie Boys to protect them.**

Elizabeth grew up in Martinaise; Evrart Claire paid for her to study law, and she came back as the Union's lawyer and fixer. When you go to interview Titus Hardie in the Whirling-in-Rags, she takes charge of the room, reminds you the Union does not recognise your authority, and steps in whenever your questions get too close.

She has no patience for your drinking or your antics and gives you a sarcastic nickname based on your worst moments.

See also: [Union](#union), [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Easy Leo](#easy-leo)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She is the same young woman you may have met earlier posing as a gardener by the Whirling-in-Rags; she was watching you. She can be killed in the shootout at the Tribunal.

</details>

### Ernö Pasternak

*Also: Pasternak*

**The most famous false innocence in history: a despot fond of torture, hymns, cannons and world conquest, who was beaten by the regent of Kedra.**

Besides the true innocences, history has produced a number of counter- or false-innocences: rulers believed to have innocentic qualities, or who simply believed it of themselves. Pasternak was the most important of them. His dream of conquering the world ended in defeat and humiliation at the hands of Stepan the Despicable of Kedra, who conquered the known world in his place.

He features in the quiz book The Greatest Innocence.

See also: [Innocence](#innocence), [Sola](#sola), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei)

### Eugene

**The youngest of the Hardie Boys, a musician with a guitar pick on a cord around his neck; less brutish than the rest and quicker to think.**

Eugene sits with Titus Hardie's crew in the Whirling-in-Rags cafeteria. He is a young musician among the dockworkers, more thoughtful than most of his friends, and he tends to follow the logic of an argument even when Titus would rather he didn't.

His work boots are the same size as Glen's, which matters when you study the footprints in the yard.

See also: [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Glen](#glen), [Fat Angus](#fat-angus)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He survives the Tribunal shootout. Afterwards, with most of his friends dead, he feels he has to stay and keep the Hardie Boys going.

</details>

### Evrart Claire

*Also: Evrart*

**The head of the Martinaise dockworkers' union. Enormous, friendly and very powerful.**

Evrart effectively runs Martinaise from the harbour. He is warm, flattering and always talking about doing favours, which you will be expected to return. He is leading the strike against the Wild Pines company.

See also: [Union](#union), [Harbour](#harbour), [Wild Pines](#wild-pines), [Strike](#strike)

### Exilus Bücher

*Also: Exilus*

**One of two bylines on an essay attacking the TipTop Tournée motor race, printed in the radical left-wing magazine La Fumée.**

The other byline is Nasteb Encalada-Bernal. Conceptualization doubts anyone is really called Exilus and suspects a pseudonym.

See also: [Nasteb Encalada-Bernal](#nasteb-encalada-bernal)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Both names are anagrams of the real authors: Steban and his comrade Ulixes, the student communists you can meet in the Capeside Apartments.

</details>

### Fat Angus

*Also: Angus, Angie*

**A huge, wheezing, sweaty member of the Hardie Boys; his friends protect him, and he looks like the weakest link in their story.**

Angus sits among Titus Hardie's crew in the Whirling-in-Rags. He is very heavy, short of breath (he forgot his inhaler) and anxious under questioning, and the others, especially Titus, keep him out of the conversation. His friends call him Angie. His boots are size 46 and he leaves unusually deep footprints.

See also: [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Glen](#glen), [Eugene](#eugene)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He is the one most likely to crack under pressure and let slip that the Hardie Boys' story about the hanging is not the whole truth; he also knows Ruby was building a 'pale emitter'. He dies in the Tribunal shootout.

</details>

### Father Jairzinho

*Also: Jairzinho*

**Measurehead's father, a welder whom he reveres as the only man truly worthy of being called a man.**

Measurehead says his stern, distant father made him hard where his mother made him soft. He talks about him while lecturing you on manhood, and won't hear a word against him.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Frittte clerk

**The bored fifteen-year-old girl behind the counter of the Martinaise Frittte, chewing gum over a celebrity magazine; 'don't fuck with Frittte'.**

She would rather read Pop-Stars than talk to you, but she will answer questions, slowly and vaguely. She runs the shop's tare machine (it pays money for empty bottles) and sells everything from raincoats to medicine.

She keeps repeating that you should not mess with Frittte. Ask why and she casually explains that the chain has its own private army, which is why nobody robs it, and that Frittte polices itself.

See also: [Frittte](#frittte)

### Fuck the World

**One of two teenage wannabe gangsters loitering near Kim's motor carriage, named for the slogan on his jacket; his friend's jacket is ruder.**

He and his companion, named for an even cruder slogan, hang around the area near Kim's Kineema, eyeing the car and dreaming of joining the SKULLS, a gang of car thieves, and of being as cool as Cindy the SKULL. He spouts mock-nihilist philosophy about 'fucking the void' but panics the moment things get serious.

With the right approach you can talk the pair out of their slogan jackets and wear one yourself, which several characters will comment on.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Nihilism](#nihilism)

### Garte

*Also: Cafeteria Manager, Garte, the Cafeteria Manager*

**The manager of the Whirling-in-Rags. You owe him money for your room (and the damage).**

Garte runs the hostel-cafeteria where you wake up. He is tired, sarcastic and mostly wants to be paid.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [reál](#reál)

### Gary

*Also: Gary, the Cryptofascist*

**Morell the cryptozoologist's assistant: a polite, shifty pie-delivery courier whose friendly chat soon turns into racist conspiracy theories.**

Morell and his wife Lena are staying at Gary's apartment near the Whirling. You find him out in the coastal reeds with Morell, soaked, cold and desperate to go home. He claims to be a 'special courier' but admits he delivers topping pies.

The game labels him a cryptofascist: he hides his politics behind excessive courtesy, but with little prompting he explains his theory of a secret Seolite plot to control the world, while nervously eyeing Kim.

See also: [Lena](#lena), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology), [Seol](#seol), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Fascism](#fascism)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The clicking sound under his clothes is the hanged man's missing armour, which Gary took and is wearing.

</details>

### Gaston Martin

*Also: Gaston*

**René Arnoux's elderly friend and pétanque partner.**

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Pétanque](#pétanque)

### Gertrude Het

**The heroine of a Martinaise ghost story: in the summer of '44, aged seventeen, she was the first to see the Headless FALN Rider by the canal.**

Idiot Doom Spiral tells the tale for the price of a bottle. Walking home from a late shift at the harbour, Gertrude stops for a cigarette by the canal and hears hooves behind her: a headless man in a FALN tracksuit on horseback, searching for the FALN cap he lost along with his head. Legend says she needed years of psychotherapy before she could look at a horse or a tracksuit again.

### Girard

**A stranger who picks up when you dial a random number from the boardwalk payphone; your detective can prank him mercilessly.**

He answers cheerfully, and a woman with a thick Samaran accent can be heard in the background. Your detective can be astonishingly rude to him, much to Authority's embarrassment.

### Glen

*Also: Glenny*

**A big, aggressive, muscular blond Hardie Boy; he is quick to insult and quicker to lose his temper.**

Glen sits with Titus Hardie's crew in the Whirling-in-Rags. He is muscular and loud, mocks you and Kim freely and flirts clumsily with the Union's lawyer Elizabeth.

His boot size, 44, is one of the clues when you study the footprints in the yard.

See also: [Eugene](#eugene), [Fat Angus](#fat-angus), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Glen can be killed in the shootout at the tribunal; Titus later mourns him as the best friend he ever had.

</details>

### Gorący Kubek

**The Whirling-in-Rags cook, a man from Graad who speaks almost none of the local language; your detective assumes his name is Gorący Kubek.**

He smokes under the kitchen hood and cooks a big pot of borscht for the Union men, happy to pour in more vodka; he will ladle you a cup of the brew. He is friends with Mañana from the harbour gates.

The only words you catch from him are 'gorący' and 'kubek', so your Logic skill first takes them for his name, then begs you never to use it. Kim calls him a Kojko, a nickname for people from Graad.

*Real-world inspiration:* 'Gorący kubek' is Polish for 'hot mug' (also a well-known instant soup in Poland); his few understandable words, like 'barszcz' and 'wódka', are Polish.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Graad](#graad), [Garte](#garte)

### Horse-Faced Woman

**A sad-eyed uniformed patrol officer who turns up at the Whirling-in-Rags with a man in sunglasses; she clearly knows you, though you do not know her.**

She is uncomfortable around you and her stern companion, calls you Harry without explanation and mostly wants you to finish the investigation so everyone can go home. Your detective gives her this unkind nickname himself, and insulting her to her face is a reliable way to make things worse.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She is Judit Minot, an officer of Harry's own Precinct 41 assigned to his unit two months earlier; the man with sunglasses is Jean Vicquemare. She tells you her real name at the end of the case.

</details>

### Idiot Doom Spiral

*Also: Doom Spiral*

**A thirty-something ex-businessman in a filthy tracksuit who drinks on the shore of the Fishing Village and calls you 'Tequila'.**

He used to be, he says, a moderately-high-net-worth hustler in marketing, until he lost his keys one rainy night and ended up homeless on the beach with his drinking buddies. He still talks like a salesman, about 'high-concept' ideas and 'the reality situation', and he would love a business opportunity.

He is a gifted storyteller, especially of the Headless FALN Rider legend, and he has lost his jacket, which you can help him find.

See also: [Filthy Jacket](#filthy-jacket), [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Headless FALN Rider](#headless-faln-rider), [Horseback Monument](#horseback-monument), [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He saw you arrive in Martinaise during your binge, introducing yourself as 'Tequila Sunset', and watched you drive your motor carriage off the jetty and through the ice.

</details>

### Jean Vicquemare

*Also: Jean, Vicquemare*

**An officer from Harry's own Precinct 41 who has very little patience left for him.**

Jean has worked with Harry for years and has seen the worst of him. He is bitter, blunt and tired of covering for his colleague.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Judit Minot](#judit-minot)

### Jeanne-Marie Beaulieu

*Also: Jeannie, Jeanne-Marie*

**The woman René Arnoux and Gaston Martin both loved for most of their lives; Gaston calls her 'sweet Jeannie'.**

She appears in an old photo of the three friends at a parade some sixty years ago. Gaston says she was the finest woman in all of Revachol and the only one besides him who could stand René.

She kept leaving one man for the other but never stayed long enough to marry either. She died peacefully of pneumonia two winters ago, at 79, and René still bristles when Gaston talks about her.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

### Joseph Mills

**A late RCM officer from the cases in your ledger; he couldn't solve the 'Murder at the Hookah Parlour', so the case was handed to you.**

Your damaged ledger recalls that Mills worked the hookah parlour case alone for weeks and called it the toughest case he had ever had, until the captain lost patience. He later died in unrelated circumstances: beaten to death by Villalobos gang members while answering a call with his partner. The ledger's verdict is blunt: he was not a good detective.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Joyce Messier

*Also: Joyce, Joyce L. Messier, Rejoyce, Rejoyce Leyton, Rejoyce Leyton-Messier*

**The negotiator sent by the Wild Pines company to deal with the dockworkers' strike.**

Joyce is wealthy, sharp and surprisingly candid. She is one of the best people in the game to ask about Revachol's politics and history, how the city is governed and who holds power in Martinaise.

See also: [Wild Pines](#wild-pines), [Strike](#strike), [Mercenaries](#mercenaries)

### João Paolo Salomao Lopez de Fuego

*Also: Lopez de Fuego*

**The author of 'The Greatest Innocence', a huge, heavy history book about the innocences sold at the Martinaise bookstore.**

The bookseller recommends his book as an important look at history, religion and 'innocentic power'. Having read it, you can summarise his argument for the student communists in the apartment building.

See also: [Innocence](#innocence), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The book argues that Dolores Dei was the greatest innocence of all.

</details>

### Judit Minot

*Also: Judit, Minot*

**A patrol officer of Precinct 41 who works alongside Jean Vicquemare.**

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare)

### Jules Pidieu

*Also: Oldboy, Communications Officer Jules Pidieu*

**Precinct 41's radio operator, nicknamed 'Oldboy'; he answers when you call your home station from the radio in Kim's motor carriage.**

Jules works the relay booth at the 41st Precinct, a job he inherited from his late brother, known as 'Cardiac' Pidieu. He talks in radio codes ('10-4', 'Over') and tries hard to stay professional.

Calling in about your lost gun and badge draws a crowd of your colleagues around his desk, and he ends up relaying their jokes and insults to you. Underneath it all he is quietly decent: he may offer to sit on his report for a few days.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Kineema](#kineema), [Mack Torson](#mack-torson), [Chester McLaine](#chester-mclaine)

### Keith and Guy Joost

**Twin curators at the Wompty-Dompty-Dom Centre, an art venue in Vredefort, Oranje; Trant Heidelstam drops their names as if everyone knows them.**

Trant mentions them while recalling an exhibition he consulted on ten years ago, when Paul Ockermann was head curator and before the twins joined the team. Even Encyclopedia has no idea who they are.

See also: [Oranje](#oranje)

### Kim Kitsuragi

*Also: Kim, Kitsuragi, Lieutenant Kitsuragi*

**Your partner on the case: a calm, methodical RCM lieutenant from Precinct 57.**

Kim was assigned to work the hanging with you. He is precise, patient, keeps meticulous notes and treats you with dry professionalism even at your worst. He drives a Kineema motor carriage, which he loves. He is of Seolite descent, which some people in Martinaise are openly hostile about.

See also: [Precinct 57](#precinct-57), [Kineema](#kineema), [Seol](#seol), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Klaasje

*Also: Klaasje Amandou, Miss Oranje Disco Dancer, Katarzine Alasije, Klaasje (Miss Oranje Disco Dancer), Miss Oranje*

**A charismatic woman staying on the Whirling's top floor, often smoking on her balcony.**

Klaasje is witty, worldly and hard to read. Her balcony overlooks the yard where the body was found.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Oranje](#oranje), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She was with the victim on the night he died and has been hiding her part in events. She is not entirely who she claims to be.

</details>

### Lamby

**Little Lily's stuffed lamb in the Fishing Village: tattered, missing an eye button, and her best friend.**

Lily holds Lamby up to meet you, presses him to your cheek and covers his ears when grown-ups swear. She claims Lamby has lived a long life and was once a 'revolutionary', if she can get the word out.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

### Lena

*Also: Lena, the Cryptozoologist's wife, Cryptozoologist's wife*

**A kind, elderly woman at the Whirling-in-Rags, married to a cryptozoologist.**

Lena loves to talk about cryptids, especially the legendary Insulindian Phasmid her husband is searching for. She is one of the gentlest people in Martinaise.

See also: [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

### Lilienne

*Also: Lilienne, the Net Picker, Lilienne Carter, Net Picker, Mademoiselle Lilienne*

**A sharp-tongued, independent woman in the fishing village who picks fish from the nets, carries a sword and raises three children.**

Lilienne grew up by the sea and works on the village's fishing nets. She is dry, teasing and hard to impress, and her politics lean towards a rough Revacholian patriotism. Her three 'monsters' are a pair of twin boys and a little girl, Little Lily.

Evrart Claire needs her signature, and that of Isobel the old washerwoman, for a construction project in the village, so she is one of the people you may be sent to ask. If you hit it off, she may invite you to walk out to Land's End with her.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Little Lily](#little-lily), [Lilienne's Twins](#liliennes-twins), [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

At Land's End she shares her thoughts about the sun as a power that favours everyone, and she may give you her sabre.

</details>

### Lilienne's Twins

*Also: Lilienne's Twin, Lilienne's Other Twin, Netpicker's Twins*

**Lilienne's young twin boys, who play outside in the fishing village kicking stones and answer questions in confusing half-sentences.**

The scruffy boys are Little Lily's older brothers. They chatter about the island out in the bay (an old seafort you can reach by raft) and about lights and fires there, and admit they got into trouble for repeating bad words they heard as voices 'in their head'.

See also: [Lilienne](#lilienne), [Little Lily](#little-lily), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Their talk about someone who stays on the island, and about the strange voices, are early hints at secrets you uncover later.

</details>

### Little Lily

**Lilienne's youngest child, a girl of about four or five who sits by the fire in the family's house in the fishing village, cuddling her toy lamb.**

Lily is cheerful and chatty, adores her mother and carries a battered stuffed lamb called Lamby, which she insists was a 'revolutionary'. She has trouble saying her l's and r's and gets upset if you swear. A stuffed grouse hangs from the ceiling of the house, and she might let you have it.

See also: [Lilienne](#lilienne), [Lilienne's Twins](#liliennes-twins), [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Garte](#garte)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She found a pair of heavy armoured gloves in an empty house while playing hide-and-seek and buried them in her sandcastle behind the house. She can tell you where to dig them up.

</details>

### Mack Torson

*Also: Sergeant Mack Torson, Sergeant Torson, Torson*

**A loud, crude sergeant at Precinct 41, nicknamed 'The Torso'; he leads the laughter when you call the station about your lost gun and badge.**

Torson is a muscular, bald officer who dresses in jeans and leather and is always paired with his partner, Satellite-Officer Chester McLaine. He mostly turns up in Esprit de Corps visions of the station, swapping dirty stories and jokes about you and your colleagues.

Judit Minot considers Torson and McLaine unfit to run a wing of the station on their own.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Chester McLaine](#chester-mclaine), [Jules Pidieu](#jules-pidieu), [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps)

### Man on water lock

**A burly man by the canal water lock, slicing salami while his friend Barry the Butcher is stranded on the other side.**

A huge billboard has fallen into the canal and jammed the water lock, which cuts off the rest of the coast (the fishing village, an old fish market and a strange church) until it is fixed. He tells you a canal crew will restart it on Wednesday morning at 07:15 and invites you to work the controls when the time comes.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Morell](#morell)

### Man with Sunglasses

**A sarcastic blond man in sunglasses who sits in the Whirling-in-Rags cafeteria with a woman; he seems to know you and is furious about it.**

He wears his sunglasses indoors, calls you 'superstar' and mocks your drinking and your police work, but keeps telling you to go and solve the hanging. He proposes odd 'thought experiments' about a hypothetical station and partnership, and gets visibly hurt when they go nowhere.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare), [Judit Minot](#judit-minot), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

He is Jean Vicquemare, your own partner from Precinct 41, in a blond wig and sunglasses, keeping an eye on you; the woman with him is Judit Minot. You don't recognise either of them.

</details>

### Marielle Charpentier

*Also: Real Estate Agent*

**A brisk, harried agent from Martinaise Realty Associates whom you can meet at night in the apartment building, cleaning up a trashed flat.**

She is getting an apartment ready for a new lease after its tenant ran off owing three months' rent. To make matters worse, someone left the neighbouring door open and there is now a hole in the wall, so both flats are unrentable.

She insists Martinaise has a bright future of artists and radiocomputer wizards, shows you her papers to prove she isn't breaking in, and clearly wants to get back to La Delta as soon as possible.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise)

### Measurehead

*Also: Jean-Luc Measurehead, Jean-Luc*

**A towering man who guards the harbour gate for the Union and lectures anyone who will listen on 'race theory'.**

Measurehead (first name Jean-Luc) is a hulking man who explains the world through pseudo-scientific racial theories. The game presents his ideas as absurd and wrong, and lets you argue with him.

*Real-world inspiration:* His name points at phrenology and craniometry: discredited 19th-century pseudosciences that 'measured heads' to justify racism.

See also: [Fascism](#fascism)

### Measurehead's Babe

*Also: Measurehead's Babes*

**One of the three admiring young women who hang around Measurehead at the harbour gates, chiming in to praise him.**

You cannot talk to her directly, but she adds her own comments during conversations with Measurehead, swooning over his speeches, agreeing with his race theories and occasionally asking him to go easier on you.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead), [Harbour](#harbour)

### Mico the Kebab

**A man Evrart Claire mentions, supposedly famous for once killing a man with a kebab.**

Moments after naming him, Evrart can't remember whether the man was actually called Mico, Bogowir, Jakub or Konrad.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Evrart brings him up to vouch for Acele, one of the young people at the church, who he says is Mico's daughter and therefore tough enough to run a nightclub.

</details>

### Mikael Heidelstam

*Also: Mikael*

**Trant Heidelstam's shy little son, who clutches a würm-themed colouring book and would rather climb ladders than hear about history.**

He hides behind his father's coat on the boardwalk, begs to climb the ladder next to the mural, and cheerfully reveals that his dad spends four hours every evening fighting with sticks in a special room, wearing a special costume.

See also: [Trant Heidelstam](#trant-heidelstam)

### Moneyman

**A background figure on the Whirling-in-Rags' upper floor who keeps muttering investment platitudes about money.**

You can't hold a conversation with him; as you pass, he offers lines like 'You gotta spend money to make money' and 'Money is actually all about trust'.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism)

### Morell

*Also: Morell, the Cryptozoologist, Poor Morell*

**Lena's husband, a passionate cryptozoologist hunting the Insulindian Phasmid in the reeds on the coast west of Martinaise.**

Morell came from Jamrock with his wife Lena and his assistant Gary after a credible-sounding sighting of the phasmid by some teenagers. He has set locust-baited traps, cleverly designed by Lena, in the reeds along the coast. Since the broken water lock cut off the coast, he has been camped out there for days, and Lena is worried.

He is touchy about mainstream scientists who refuse to take cryptozoology seriously, and insists he is a dispassionate scientist, not an adventurer.

See also: [Lena](#lena), [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology), [Phasmid Trap](#phasmid-trap), [Man on water lock](#man-on-water-lock)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The 'sighting' that brought him to Martinaise may have a much more ordinary explanation than a cryptid.

</details>

### Mysterious Pair of Eyes

**Intense blue eyes peering at you through a gap in the harbour gates, belonging to a mocking voice that seems to have watched everything you have done.**

The voice behind the gates insults you as a 'piggy' and a 'copper', lists your embarrassing or violent acts around Martinaise, and insists the eyes of Martinaise are always watching. He refuses to say who he is and denies being a lookout for anyone.

See also: [Harbour](#harbour), [Pig](#pig)

### Nasteb Encalada-Bernal

*Also: Nasteb*

**One of two bylines on an essay attacking the TipTop Tournée motor race, printed in the radical left-wing magazine La Fumée.**

The other byline is Exilus Bücher. Both names look suspiciously like pseudonyms.

See also: [Exilus Bücher](#exilus-bücher)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

'Nasteb' is an anagram of Steban, the student communist who runs a secret reading group in the Capeside Apartments.

</details>

### Nix Gottlieb

*Also: Dr. Gottlieb, Dr. Nix Gottlieb, Gottlieb, Nix Fucklieb*

**Precinct 41's station medic and surgeon; you can phone him from Kim's motor carriage, though his bedside manner is brutal.**

Gottlieb runs the station's lazareth (infirmary) and makeshift morgue. If you call him about your symptoms, he diagnoses you with weary contempt, then tells you to stop drinking and take magnesium and vitamin D.

Station gossip says he once removed an officer's appendix in the middle of a party while reeking of schnapps, did a fine job, and had no memory of it afterwards.

See also: [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps), [Kineema](#kineema), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Ptolemy Pryce](#ptolemy-pryce)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Late in the game, after Harry is badly wounded, Gottlieb operates on him in his room while he sleeps. Cuno, who sticks around at Harry's bedside, remembers the doctor as 'Nix Fucklieb'.

</details>

### Noid

**A jittery young speedfreak and tinkerer from Faubourg, part of Andre's group of ravers trying to set up a club in the old church.**

Noid spends his time hunched over a toolbox, sorting screws and admiring the church's carpentry. He won't talk properly until he has 'got a reading on your sine', and then holds forth on the 'hard core' philosophy: beats, bright lights, suspicion of nearly everything, and deep distrust of left-right politics and its 'pig/wheat paradigm'.

If you wear the Horrific Necktie, he is very impressed by it.

See also: [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Horrific Necktie](#horrific-necktie)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

With a suitable transceiver and his tinkering, you can attempt to contact the Coalition warship Archer.

</details>

### Novelty Dicemaker

*Also: Neha, dicemaker*

**Neha, a bird-like jeweller and repair expert in the Doomed Commercial Area who makes custom dice for role-playing games.**

Sitting among her tools and precious stones, Neha will design a die for any role-playing system or idea you describe, for a few réal, ready in eight hours. She makes only one per customer.

She knows the sad history of the building's many failed businesses (a radio game studio, an ice cream parlour, an atelier, a ski company) and is curious whether you believe the place is cursed. She also hands you a pair of lucky dice as a gift.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Plaisance](#plaisance)

### Paledriver

**An elderly long-haul lorry driver stuck in the traffic jam; years of crossing the pale have left her lost in dreams and other people's memories.**

She calls herself one of the best camionneurs around, driving pale routes no one else will take, with her lorry carried in the hold of an airship. She leans on the railing listening to her radio, clutching a photo of the old Mesque film star Gabriel Buenguerro, and drifts back to the golden age of Mesque cinema and memories she knows are not her own.

She calls you 'lawman' or 'xerife' and is equal parts dreamy and scornful. Talking to her is a good way to learn how the pale affects the mind.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Western Plain](#western-plain), [Lorryman](#lorryman), [Aerostatic](#aerostatic)

### Paul Ockermann

**The former head curator of the Wompty-Dompty-Dom Centre for Contemporary Arts in Vredefort, Oranje; Trant Heidelstam drops his name.**

Trant mentions him while recalling an exhibition he consulted on about ten years ago, which drew on 'Lagerman's notion of memory'. The name means nothing to you.

See also: [Oranje](#oranje)

### Pissf****t

**A blond would-be gangster loitering by Kim's motor carriage with his friend 'Fuck the World', named after the word painted on his jacket.**

He and his friend admire the Kineema, dream of joining the SKULLS (which they call the most vicious gang of the Besmertnyé) and worship a girl called Cindy the SKULL. He has a lofty nihilist explanation for the slur on his back, but crumbles at the first sign of real confrontation.

With enough pressure you can make him hand over his jacket, which you can then wear.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Plaisance

**The anxious, superstitious owner of the bookstore 'Crime, Romance, and Biographies of Famous People' in the Doomed Commercial Area.**

Plaisance wears a moss-green outfit and little gold spectacles and keeps fiddling with her pendant. She insists her shop has wonderful 'magnetic' energy and denies the rumoured curse on the building, yet talks of spirits and wards and becomes frantic if you go near the curtained back room.

She sells novels, maps, board games and esoteric books, and dislikes many of the titles she stocks. Her daughter Annette stands outside on the plaza selling books for her.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Man from Hjelmdall](#man-from-hjelmdall), [Medicinal Purposes of the Pale](#medicinal-purposes-of-the-pale), [Novelty Dicemaker](#novelty-dicemaker)

### President Knezhinisky

*Also: Knezhinisky, Sapormat 'Sport' Knezhinisky, Uncle Sport*

**Sapormat 'Sport' Knezhinisky, long-ruling president of the communist People's Republic of Samara, famous for his fitness obsession.**

Encyclopedia says he is well into his third decade in power and known abroad for three things: brutal repression of dissent, monumental corruption and a near-pathological obsession with physical fitness. Like all Samaran men over forty, he wears a spectacular walrus moustache.

The magazine La Fumée reports that 'Uncle Sport', as his people call him, has made bear wrestling the national sport as part of a public fitness drive. Steban and his reading group insist he absorbs a book's content just by staring at its cover.

See also: [People's Republic of Samara](#peoples-republic-of-samara), [Communism](#communism)

### Ptolemy Pryce

*Also: Captain Pryce, Captain Ptolemy Pryce, Pryce, Ptolemaios Pryce*

**The captain of Precinct 41, Harry's home station: one of the most respected men in the RCM, glimpsed only through Esprit de Corps visions.**

Esprit de Corps shows him as a bald, bespectacled man of 58 who keeps the station's ledger by the light of a green desk lamp and rides home on a piebald horse. Kim says everyone respects the 41st because it has Captain Pryce, and Judit Minot explains that he is the son of the old Pryce, one of the founders of the RCM.

Jean Vicquemare has had to explain Harry's behaviour to him more than once.

See also: [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare), [Judit Minot](#judit-minot), [Nix Gottlieb](#nix-gottlieb), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Jean Vicquemare complains that he has had to explain Harry's behaviour to Pryce more than once. In the closing scene, Pryce tells the precinct medic Nix Gottlieb that Harry is 'our man' and will pull through.

</details>

### Racist lorry driver

**A lorry driver stuck in the traffic jam near the harbour, waiting on a load of apples; he greets newcomers with 'Welcome to Revachol!' and has ugly views on race.**

He calls himself a camionneur, a father of three and a Revacholian patriot, and boasts of an ancient, privileged guild of lorrymen. Given an audience, he lectures on 'race theory' and the supposed superiority of Occidentals, and he is openly hostile to Kim.

He complains that cargo is being looted from the stranded lorries at night and points the finger at Siileng, the street vendor down by the canal.

See also: [Siileng](#siileng), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Fascism](#fascism), [Strike](#strike)

### Raphaël Ambrosius Costeau

*Also: Raphaël A. Costeau, Mr. Costeau, Raphael Costeau*

**A grand fake name that Conceptualization can invent for you when Kim first asks who you are. You can keep using it around Martinaise.**

Having forgotten your real name, you can let Conceptualization dream up something that sounds 'solemn and historical'. Kim plays along with a straight face, the Horrific Necktie loves it, and most people take it with a pinch of salt. Insisting on it can even earn you a Thought.

See also: [Conceptualization](#conceptualization), [Tequila Sunset](#tequila-sunset), [Horrific Necktie](#horrific-necktie)

### René Arnoux

*Also: René, Rene Arnoux*

**An old veteran who plays pétanque. He fought for the royalists during the Revolution.**

René is proud, grumpy and still loyal to the old monarchy that the communist revolution overthrew decades ago. He plays pétanque with his friend Gaston.

See also: [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revolution](#revolution), [Pétanque](#pétanque)

### Rosemary

**A jolly drunk in the fishing village who runs a tiny shop of cigarettes, cheap pilsner, speed and a suspicious bottle of blue 'spirits'.**

He hangs out with Idiot Doom Spiral as part of what they call the Union of Moribund Alcoholics, and calls you 'buddy-boy' and 'legend'. He says he used to teach high school biology.

His prize item is a bottle of blue medicinal spirits, nearly pure alcohol, which he claims to have swiped from a doctor's office (after throwing out the preserved snakes). He keeps forgetting whether it costs 300 réal or 3.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [reál](#reál), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

### Scab Leader

**The loudest of the strikebreakers outside the harbour gates: a huge, square-jawed man chanting 'Right to work!' at the striking dockworkers.**
