Kim explains that Internal Affairs cross-examines officers closely, looking for inconsistencies in their stories, so it is hard to cover for a colleague. He brings it up when you consider helping yourself to the hanged man's gear, and again when a case threatens to get messy.

*Real-world inspiration:* Internal affairs divisions of real-world police forces.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### International Collaboration Police

*Also: ICP*

**The ICP: a worldwide police service that coordinates multinational investigations and keeps the biggest database in the world.**

Encyclopedia calls it an interisolary law enforcement service and the crown jewel of the Moralintern, alongside EPIS and the Coalition Government. The Moralintern set it up partly to track military technology: it keeps detailed records of everything licensed arms manufacturers produce.

The RCM can request ICP records through a precinct radio operator, and Joyce Messier suggests using its database for background checks.

*Real-world inspiration:* Interpol.

See also: [Moralintern](#moralintern), [Coalition](#coalition), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Juvenile Crime Unit

**The RCM unit that deals with young offenders; Kim spent over fifteen years there before moving to homicide.**

Kim brings it up when young people try to shock him: after that many years with juvenile delinquents, he says, he can 'do' age-inappropriate.

If you play pinball with him, he admits his last juvenile job was infiltrating a pinball ring, which meant training for nine months to become a pinball champion. The job succeeded and he was moved to homicide.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Krenel

*Also: Downwell*

**An Oranjese private military company. Wild Pines has a handful of its mercenaries in Martinaise because of the strike.**

Joyce Messier says three Krenel contractors came to Martinaise; they report to her only now and then, and they do not take her orders. She describes a firm with a thousand men on its payroll that could send in gunships if things escalate.

The company changes names: Joyce believes it used to be called Downwell.

See also: [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Mercenaries](#mercenaries), [Oranje](#oranje), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Wild Pines](#wild-pines)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The hanged man behind the Whirling was a Krenel mercenary, and Joyce fears his squadmates will pick their own suspect if the police don't find one first.

</details>

### La Puta Madre

*Also: the Madre, Madre, Padre Madre*

**The boss of a major Jamrock gang that makes and traffics drugs, and by extension the gang itself, often just called 'the Madre'.**

Kim mentions the Madre alongside other gangs such as Ahura Mazda and the besmerties, and calls their 'cool' names a dark parody. People usually wince and whisper when they say the name. Cuno boasts about banging for the Madre, and Kim suspects the gang is behind a smuggling operation you come across.

Rumours that Harry, and even Kim, are on La Puta Madre's take come up more than once.

See also: [Jamrock](#jamrock), [Cuno](#cuno)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Ruby, the Instigator, believes Harry is La Puta Madre's 'peone', his 'Human Can Opener', and fled Martinaise rather than be questioned by him. She says she left the Madre for the Union to get away from the killing.

</details>

### Major Crimes Unit

**Harry's team at Precinct 41, which he leads together with Jean Vicquemare. Its job is to clear the station's backlog of cases.**

According to Jean, the unit is Harry, Jean, Judit ('Jude'), Trant Heidelstam and the police reporter Guillaume Bevy.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare), [Judit Minot](#judit-minot), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Jean says the unit has sunk to the bottom tier because of Harry, and that Bevy has already quit after losing faith in Harry's leadership.

</details>

### Mercenaries

*Also: mercenary, mercs, merc*

**Hired private soldiers brought into Martinaise on the company side of the strike.**

They come from Krenel, an Oranjese military contractor working for Wild Pines. Joyce Messier says three of its men came to Martinaise; they report to her now and then, but they do not take her orders.

See also: [Wild Pines](#wild-pines), [Strike](#strike), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The murdered man was their commander, and his men want revenge on whoever they believe killed him.

</details>

### Moralintern

*Also: Moralist International, MI*

**The 'Moralist International': the world's largest political organisation, and the power behind the Coalition that runs Revachol.**

The Moralintern presents itself as the reasonable, peaceful middle ground of world politics; in practice it protects the status quo. Moralism's colour is blue. The laws the RCM enforces come from it, and Kim admits that without it the police would be mere vigilantes.

*Real-world inspiration:* The name parodies the 'Comintern' (Communist International); what it does resembles the UN, the IMF and the EU rolled into one.

See also: [Coalition](#coalition), [Moralism](#moralism), [Innocence](#innocence)

### Parti communiste d'Insulinde

*Also: Communist Party*

**French for 'Communist Party of Insulinde': a communist party from Revachol's revolutionary days, sworn enemy of the Moralintern.**

The name is rarely heard in present-day Martinaise. It belongs to the era of the Revolution and the Commune, before the Coalition took the city.

See also: [Commune](#commune), [Revolution](#revolution), [Communism](#communism), [Moralintern](#moralintern)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Deserter, a veteran of the Commune, claims to represent the Party as its political commissar. He sees the RCM as servants of the 'Moralist International', and insists that although the army signed the surrender, the Party never did.

</details>

### Provisional Commission

**One of the Coalition's offices in Revachol; it reports on the city's slow 'transition' towards full democracy.**

Trant Heidelstam pictures the Coalition as a wheel whose hub is the Committee of Responsibility, with spokes leading to bodies such as INSURCOM, the Institute for Revacholian Culture and the Provisional Commission. Over the radio, the Coalition Warship Archer assures you that, according to the Provisional Commission, the transition is on schedule, and suggests contacting its offices if you want to get involved.

See also: [Coalition](#coalition), [Moralintern](#moralintern)

### Remote Viewers Division

*Also: RVD, RVD HQ*

**A police division for psychic detectives that Kim invents as a deadpan joke; your detective may decide it is real and that he belongs to it.**

When you insist you can sense something special about a closed trash container in the yard, Kim replies with a perfectly straight face that you should call in the Remote Viewers Division, where all detectives with extrasensory perception go. Rhetoric warns you he is being sarcastic, and if pressed Kim admits there is no such thing.

That doesn't stop your detective from bringing it up with the bookseller Plaisance, the novelty dicemaker, Steban and others, or from 'joining' it through the Remote Viewer thought.

*Real-world inspiration:* Remote viewing, the supposed psychic ability to see distant places, once studied by US military and intelligence programmes.

See also: [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Remote Viewer](#remote-viewer)

### Revachol Citizens Militia

*Also: RCM, Citizens Militia, the Militia*

**Revachol's police force. You and Kim are RCM officers.**

The RCM is a volunteer organisation that keeps order in occupied Revachol. It was formed after the Revolution, although it is disputed whether the city's citizens or the Coalition Government really founded it. It works in a legal grey zone, is understaffed, and is divided into numbered precincts; its officers hold ranks like lieutenant and yefreitor.

Its authority is limited: the laws it enforces come from the Coalition and the Moralintern, and in Martinaise the dockworkers' union acts as the de facto police.

*Real-world inspiration:* In the Soviet Union and other Eastern Bloc states, the police were officially called the 'militia' (militsiya).

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Precinct 57](#precinct-57), [Yefreitor](#yefreitor), [Esprit de Corps](#esprit-de-corps)

### Semen Retention Society

*Also: S.R.S., Semenese Race Scientists*

**Measurehead's 'S.R.S.': a closed circle of men who, he says, build up 'strategic semen reserves' through sheer iron will.**

Measurehead mentions belonging to the S.R.S. during a conversation about women, and Encyclopedia has no record of it. Your skills guess wildly at the initials (Authority suggests 'Semenese Race Scientists') before he explains, adding that only after years of emotional, psychological and spiritual 'powerlifting' can a man hope to join.

*Real-world inspiration:* Online 'semen retention' and NoFap movements.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Therriers

*Also: Therrier*

**The secret servicemen who guard an Innocence. Dolores Dei was always surrounded by hers.**

Encyclopedia recalls that her Therriers placed a white gold wreath on her head at her crowning, which was held on an emptied plaza for fear of assassination. A row of them stands guard around her on the church's stained glass window.

See also: [Innocence](#innocence), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity)

### Union

*Also: Dockworkers' Union, dockworkers union, the Union, Débardeurs, Débardeurs Union*

**The Martinaise dockworkers' union, led by Evrart Claire. The most powerful force in the district.**

The union controls the harbour and, in practice, much of Martinaise. It is currently on strike against the Wild Pines company, which has brought shipping to a standstill.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Strike](#strike), [Harbour](#harbour), [Wild Pines](#wild-pines), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys)

### Union of Moribund Alcoholics

**Idiot Doom Spiral's grand name for himself and his permanently drunk companions in the fishing village.**

By his own account the 'Union' gets its drink on around the clock, which is how its members claim to have witnessed some of the odder events around Martinaise. When asked about his fellow members, he says they are exactly what they look like. His friend Rosemary is one of them.

See also: [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Union](#union), [Ulla Deponte](#ulla-deponte)

### Wild Pines

*Also: Wild Pines Group*

**A large logistics company whose cargo is stuck because of the dockworkers' strike.**

Wild Pines Group is one of Revachol's original 'indotribes': companies granted royal monopolies by the king centuries ago. It has since grown into a giant with 22 terminals and about 72,000 employees, and the Martinaise harbour terminal is one of them.

It has sent Joyce Messier to negotiate an end to the strike.

*Real-world inspiration:* Multinational shipping and logistics corporations.

See also: [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Strike](#strike), [Union](#union), [Mercenaries](#mercenaries)

## Politics & Ideas

### Bourgeoisie

*Also: bourgeois*

**The owning class: people who own businesses, property and capital. The opposite of the proletariat.**

See also: [Proletariat](#proletariat), [Petite bourgeoisie](#petite-bourgeoisie), [Communism](#communism)

### Centrism

*Also: centrist, centrists*

**Politics of the middle ground, avoiding the left and the right. In the game, that's Moralism.**

See also: [Moralism](#moralism)

### Class consciousness

**Workers' awareness that they share interests as a class, and that those clash with their employers'.**

See also: [Proletariat](#proletariat), [Communism](#communism)

### Communism

*Also: communist, communists, Mazovianism, Mazovian socio-economics, Mazovian thought*

**In Elysium: the ideology founded by Kras Mazov, aiming for a classless society where workers own everything.**

Communism, also called Mazovianism or 'Mazovian socio-economics', briefly ruled Revachol after the Revolution, until the Coalition crushed it. In today's Revachol communists are a defeated, nostalgic minority who still believe a better world is possible. Its symbol is a white star in a wreath of antlers.

The game treats communism with both sympathy and mockery, as it does every ideology.

*Real-world inspiration:* Marxism and the communist movements of the 19th and 20th centuries.

See also: [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov), [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune), [Proletariat](#proletariat), [Political alignment](#political-alignment)

### Dialectics

*Also: dialectic, dialectical, dialectical materialism*

**A way of reasoning in which opposing forces clash and produce something new. Central to Marxist theory.**

See also: [Communism](#communism)

### Econoclard

*Also: Econoclards, Mazovian Economists*

**A sneering nickname for the Mazovian Economists, a moderate communist school that wants to reach communism gradually through managed economic growth.**

Encyclopedia notes the nickname is an extra-insulting version of an already insulting label. Steban, the student communist, mocks them as bean-counters obsessed with forecasting models, liberals in disguise who love spreadsheets.

Together with the Gottwald School, they are the fashionable left-wing schools that leave his own infra-materialist reading group short of recruits.

See also: [Gottwald School](#gottwald-school), [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov)

### Emergencies Act

*Also: Emergency Act, Emergencies Act of Trade and Aliments, Emergency Trade and Aliments Acts*

**The sweeping emergency law the Coalition put in place over post-war Revachol. The RCM's own authority comes from the same source.**

Encyclopedia calls it the cornerstone of post-war Revachol, an octopus straddling the city. Joyce Messier cites it as what lets her keep company business to herself, and it is loose enough that 'donations' to police officers are allowed as long as they are logged with the precinct.

Repealing it would mean throwing off the Coalition itself, which also leases the RCM the right to police West Revachol.

See also: [Coalition](#coalition), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Revachol](#revachol)

### Fascism

*Also: fascist, fascists, fascha, Revacholian nationalism*

**In Elysium: aggressive Revacholian nationalism, nostalgic for past glory and hostile to outsiders.**

Picking fascist options makes your detective rant about national greatness and other races. The game frames this path as pathetic and ugly, and characters respond accordingly.

*Real-world inspiration:* Real-world fascism and ethnic nationalism.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead), [Political alignment](#political-alignment), [Revanchism](#revanchism)

### Girl Child Revolution

*Also: Girl Child Communism*

**A poetic, almost ghostly personification of the revolution to come, from communist literature; your detective may see her in children.**

Trant Heidelstam traces the figure to Lu Jiatun's 'Mirova Cycle', whose returning character, Girl Child Communism, is a ghostly apparition. Your detective can greet little Lily in the fishing village as 'Girl Child Revolution', and a communist vision on the boardwalk sends you off to find Girl Child Revolution and Girl Child Communism.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Revolution](#revolution)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Very late in the game, the old communist you meet says he has spent all these years waiting for her: Girl Child Revolution.

</details>

### Gottwald School

**A fashionable school of left-wing critical theory which holds that intellectuals can only critique capitalism from inside it, not overthrow it.**

Along with the Econoclards, it is one of the leading schools on the left today. Steban, the student communist, despises it: to him its followers look and talk like communists but are really depressed liberals who have read too many books. A Gottwaldian, he says, would tell you that modern society can only repackage its shared history in ever more ludicrous forms.

*Real-world inspiration:* The Frankfurt School of critical theory.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Econoclard](#econoclard), [Gottwald](#gottwald)

### Hegemony

*Also: hegemonic*

**Dominance, especially the cultural kind: when one group's ideas seem like plain common sense to everyone.**

See also: [Moralism](#moralism)

### Ignus Nilsen

*Also: Apocalyptic Shrike, Evangelist of the Revolution, Nilsen*

**A communist revolutionary and theorist, Kras Mazov's close comrade; admirers call him the Evangelist of the Revolution, enemies the Apocalyptic Shrike.**

Nilsen was a leading political and military figure of the Antecentennial Revolution, second only to Mazov himself in fame. Admirers called him the 'Evangelist of the Revolution'. Enemies called him the 'Apocalyptic Shrike' after he ordered thousands of prisoners of war impaled during the retreat from Graad to Samara.

He argued that revolutionary thought radiates from the brain as a kind of energy, plasm, that might one day reshape matter itself; his followers turned this into the school called infra-materialism. His reputation has faded in most of the world, but the People's Republic of Samara honours him as a founding father, and Steban's communist reading group studies him closely.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov), [People's Republic of Samara](#peoples-republic-of-samara), [Plasm](#plasm), [Revolution](#revolution)

### Kras Mazov

*Also: Mazov, Mazovian, Mazovians, Bust of Kras Mazov, Comrade Mazov, Kras, Kras Mazov Portrait*

**The founding thinker of communism in Elysium. 'Mazovian' means communist.**

*Real-world inspiration:* Karl Marx.

See also: [Communism](#communism)

### Laissez-faire

**French for 'let it be': the belief that government should leave the economy alone.**

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism)

### Le Retour

*Also: The Return, the Return*

**French for 'The Return': a vague rumour, whispered all over Revachol West, that some great event will one day bring something back.**

Kim says that in Revachol West someone is always whispering about Le Retour. Nobody agrees on what exactly will return; Conceptualization sees it as an empty vessel waiting to be filled, since something always returns from the past.

The huge graffito that appears on the plaza, promising 'one day I will return to your side', makes you wonder whether it is connected.

See also: [Revachol](#revachol), [Klaasje](#klaasje)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Klaasje is waiting for the Return. She calls it part urban myth and part political science, popular in industrial espionage circles, and deliberately vague: it could be the return of the king, the nation, communism, or a return on investment.

</details>

### Lumpenproletariat

*Also: lumpen*

**In Marxist theory: the poorest underclass of drifters and petty criminals, outside organised labour.**

See also: [Proletariat](#proletariat)

### Means of production

**The things used to make wealth: factories, tools, land, ports. Communists want workers to own them.**

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Proletariat](#proletariat)

### Moralism

*Also: moralist, moralists, Moralist*

**In Elysium: cautious, compromise-seeking centrism. The official ideology of the Moralintern and the Coalition.**

Moralists want stability, slow reform and no sudden moves. The game portrays it as reasonable-sounding but complacent: it keeps the world as it is.

*Real-world inspiration:* Liberal centrism and technocracy.

See also: [Moralintern](#moralintern), [Coalition](#coalition), [Centrism](#centrism), [Political alignment](#political-alignment)

### Petite bourgeoisie

*Also: petit bourgeois, petty bourgeois, petit-bourgeois, petty bourgeoisie*

**The 'small' owning class: shopkeepers and small business owners. Often used as an insult by communists.**

See also: [Bourgeoisie](#bourgeoisie), [Proletariat](#proletariat)

### Plasm

**Ignus Nilsen's idea of ideological energy: revolutionary belief radiating out of the brain, perhaps strong enough to bend physical reality.**

According to Nilsen, the brain works like a transmitter, constantly giving off waves of politically charged energy. If people believe fervently enough, this plasm might start to influence the material world around it. His followers built a school of communism on the idea, infra-materialism, and argue about what a society with the highest possible level of plasm ('Communism Above Reality') would look like.

You can read all about it in the guide A Brief Look at Infra-Materialism, and Steban's reading group takes it very seriously.

See also: [Ignus Nilsen](#ignus-nilsen), [Communism](#communism), [Dialectics](#dialectics)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Steban and his comrade Uli have been testing whether the plasm between the two of them is enough to hold up a few matchboxes.

</details>

### Plutocracy

*Also: plutocrat, plutocrats, plutocratic*

**Rule by the rich. A plutocrat is someone whose wealth gives them power.**

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism)

### Proletariat

*Also: proletarian, proletarians*

**The working class: people who earn a living by selling their labour, like the dockworkers.**

See also: [Bourgeoisie](#bourgeoisie), [Communism](#communism), [Lumpenproletariat](#lumpenproletariat)

### Puncher and Wattmann

**A pair of political theorists studied by the student communists' secret reading group; they wrote the multi-volume 'Innocence of Capital'.**

Steban, the student communist, counts their work among the foundational texts of Mazovian theory, although he stresses they are not infra-materialists. He quotes them as saying that communism is a secular version of Perikarnassian theology: it swaps faith in the divine for faith in humanity's future.

His sidekick, the Echo Maker, adds that in 'Innocence of Capital' they argue communists should own as little capital as possible, so that it doesn't dilute their thinking. Steban thinks they should be required reading.

See also: [Innocence of Capital](#innocence-of-capital), [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov)

### Race Enigma

*Also: Great Race Enigma*

**Measurehead's name for the great mystery at the heart of his race theory, which he claims to have solved and refuses to share.**

Measurehead insists the Race Enigma runs much deeper than skin colour and calls it the great mystery of living organisms. If you sit through his lecture on the race types and ask whether you now understand his theory, he scoffs that you haven't even put the mystery into words yet, let alone solved it.

To get there he tells you to internalize what you have heard, which adds a race-theory Thought to your Thought Cabinet and a task to come back to him with your answer.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead), [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Internalize](#internalize)

### Reactionary

*Also: reactionaries*

**Someone who wants to turn back social or political change and return to an older order.**

See also: [Fascism](#fascism), [Royalists](#royalists)

### Revanchism

*Also: revanchist, revanchists*

**Nationalist desire to take revenge for a past defeat and win back what was lost.**

See also: [Fascism](#fascism)

### Revisionism

*Also: revisionist, revisionists*

**Communist insult for watering down the true doctrine. Also: rewriting history to suit an agenda.**

See also: [Communism](#communism)

### Seolite Conspiracy

**Gary the cryptofascist's theory that Seolite agents secretly use superior technology to manipulate governments and corporations worldwide.**

Gary claims that while Seol keeps itself closed off, its agents climb the ladders of power on other isolas, blackmailing and turning officials with advanced spy technology. He begs you not to let Kim know you know.

If you confront Kim about it, he tells you once, calmly, that there is no Seolite Conspiracy, and that even if there were, he would not be part of it. He was born and raised in Revachol.

*Real-world inspiration:* 'Yellow Peril' fears and conspiracy theories about East Asian infiltration.

See also: [Seol](#seol), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Fascism](#fascism)

### Strike

*Also: strikes, on strike*

**Workers refusing to work to pressure their employer. The Martinaise dockworkers are on strike.**

The dockworkers' union has stopped work in the harbour in a dispute with the Wild Pines company. The strike shapes everything in Martinaise, including how people react to the murder.

See also: [Union](#union), [Wild Pines](#wild-pines), [Scab](#scab), [Picket line](#picket-line)

### Ultraliberalism

*Also: ultraliberal, ultraliberals, Ultraliberal*

**In Elysium: extreme free-market capitalism. Money, deregulation and personal success above all.**

Ultraliberals believe markets should run everything and that getting rich is the highest good. Picking ultraliberal options makes your detective talk like an obsessive investor and self-help guru.

*Real-world inspiration:* Neoliberalism, libertarian economics and hustle culture.

See also: [Moralism](#moralism), [Communism](#communism), [Fascism](#fascism), [Political alignment](#political-alignment), [Laissez-faire](#laissez-faire)

### Ultras

**Nickname for the liberals who backed the Revolution instead of the monarchy, and did well out of it. Also the model name of a sleek FALN sneaker.**

Joyce Messier explains that, when the Revolution came, some wealthy liberals stayed loyal to the Constitution and the monarchy (a big mistake), while others bet on the Revolution. Those were called the Ultras, or ultraliberals, and they fared well.

Separately, a street vendor in Jamrock tries to sell you a pair of FALN 'Ultras': jet black, futuristic sneakers with a silver-lined midsole, displayed on a pedestal of old speakers.

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Revolution](#revolution), [Royalists](#royalists), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier)

### Wayfarer Act

**One of the few laws in Revachol: it protects freedom of movement and public gathering, and sharply limits what the RCM can demand of citizens.**

Under the Wayfarer Act the RCM cannot, for example, demand to see anyone's operator licence, or throw people out of public spaces unless they are disturbing public order. Together with the Emergency and Aliments Acts it defines the RCM's responsibilities in the Revachol Zone of Control; Joyce says these laws keep the city in a laissez-faire stasis that benefits foreign capital.

Kim says even schoolchildren know it, and several people you meet, Joyce among them, quote it at you.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Coalition](#coalition), [Laissez-faire](#laissez-faire), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier)

### World Republic

**The communist dream of a single republic spanning the whole world, the goal that true believers say all their work serves.**

Rhetoric reminds you that the state of your room or your paperwork doesn't matter, only your commitment to building the World Republic. You can also tell Call Me Mañana that his deeds align with it, and toast its glory elsewhere.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [World Revolution](#world-revolution), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov)

### Zone of Control

*Also: Revachol Zone of Control, zones of control*

**Revachol's current status: a territory run by the foreign Coalition, with almost no government of its own and a 'market driven' democracy.**

Lena explains it to you early on. Joyce Messier calls the Zone of Control the third incarnation of Revachol, after the king and the Commune, and says the Coalition hands the RCM crumbs of the law it took away. Only a few laws govern it, such as the Wayfarer Act and the Emergency Trade and Aliments Acts.

Its flag is signal blue, and official documents carry the words 'Revachol Zone of Control'. Kim mentions people who say it won't hold up for long.

See also: [Coalition](#coalition), [Revachol](#revachol), [Moralintern](#moralintern), [Commune](#commune), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

## History

### Beachhead

*Also: the Beachhead, Martinaise Beachhead*

**The Coalition's amphibious landing to retake Revachol from the Commune in '08; Martinaise was one of the three places where the troops came ashore.**

René Arnoux explains that the operation, called Operation Death Blow, used Martinaise as one of three footholds in the city, the others being Stella Maris and the Delta. The district was shelled first to 'soften up' the communists, which is why it is still full of bomb craters and war damage.

For Joyce Messier the Beachhead is the day the Coalition took the city, and she sees the present as a shadow cast by that day. René, a royalist veteran, hates the foreigners but calls them the lesser evil.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune), [Coalition](#coalition), [Martinaise](#martinaise), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux)

### Church raid

*Also: police raid*

**A secret police raid that shot up the old church in Martinaise some time ago and left it abandoned; nobody likes to talk about it.**

Tiago, who lives in the church, says it was abandoned after a police raid, and suddenly goes flat when asked about it. Kim heard the place was shot to pieces in a clandestine operation involving three precincts, reportedly including Harry's own Precinct 41. He knows nothing more and prefers not to pry into other districts' business.

See also: [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If you ask your colleagues from Precinct 41 about it, Jean Vicquemare admits there may have been a raid on 'some churches' because the RCM's enemies were believed to be hiding in one. He refuses to say more, telling you your security clearance is far too low right now.

</details>

### Co Hoi

*Also: Co Hoi-style, Co Hoi massacre*

**A village in South Safre made infamous by war crimes in the long proxy war between Graad and Safre; mercenaries use its name as shorthand for atrocity.**

Encyclopedia explains that the South Safre conflict has been raging for 12 years with atrocities piling up daily, most of them committed by Graad. Titus Hardie describes the dead mercenary as a man who 'had Co Hoi and Semenine written all over him'. Kim suspects that 'Co Hoi' is simply merc talk for slaughter.

See also: [Mercenaries](#mercenaries), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Klaasje](#klaasje)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

On a tape of the dead mercenary's voice he threatens to do the people at the Whirling 'Co Hoi-style'. Klaasje says he never told her he had served there, but that he lived 'his own little Co Hoi' all the same.

</details>

### Commune

*Also: Commune of Revachol, the Commune, Revacholian Commune, Communard, Communards, communard, communards, Revachol commune*

**The short-lived communist government of Revachol after the Revolution. Its members were Communards.**

The Commune of Revachol was declared in '02 and ruled the city until the Coalition invaded and destroyed it several years later. Its army was the Insulindian Citizens Militia (ICM), and the RCM may descend from it.

Communists are still called communards today.

*Real-world inspiration:* The Paris Commune of 1871, whose members were also called Communards and which was also crushed by force.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Coalition](#coalition), [Communism](#communism)

### Croix de Bravoure

*Also: Cross of Valour*

**The 'Cross of Valour', the highest battlefield medal of the old royal army. René Arnoux still wears one pinned to his uniform.**

Encyclopedia describes it as the top award for exceptional bravery in the armed forces of King Frissel the First, the king who was executed during the Revolution. The medal shows a crowned head in front of two crossed rifles, hanging from a blue striped triangle.

René says he got his for bravery, after dragging a badly wounded royal officer, Drysant, out of a disastrous battle. He also wears the Setting Sun, a separate medal given to seasoned combat veterans.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Drysant](#drysant), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revolution](#revolution)

### Dobreva and Abadanaiz

*Also: Abadanaiz, Dobreva, Dual Commissars of Revolution, Jean Abadanaiz, Julia Dobreva, Revolutionary Lovers*

**Julia Dobreva and Jean Abadanaiz, the 'Revolutionary Lovers': legendary joint commissars of the communist revolution in Insulinde.**

Dobreva came from Graad and Abadanaiz was born in Revachol. Steban, the student communist, describes her as a brilliant, charismatic comet and him as the steady partner who kept her in orbit. They served together as Dual Commissars of Revolution, and their forbidden love and disappearance when the Commune collapsed made them legends.

Joyce Messier, for her part, claims Dobreva ended up with a bullet in her head. Encyclopedia considers the pair a deep cut, but young communists in Martinaise still invoke their names.

See also: [Commune](#commune), [Communism](#communism), [Insulinde](#insulinde), [Revolution](#revolution)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

'Remember Dobreva and Abadanaiz' is the passphrase that opens the metal grille door to the student communists' secret reading group in the Capeside Apartments.

</details>

### Ecole de Contrôle Aérien

**A military training school of the Revolution's army, the Insulindian Citizens Militia. The French name means 'School of Air Control'.**

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Deserter, an old communard soldier, says he was recruited in Jamrock in '07, trained here, and assigned to emergency defence duties in '08.

</details>

### Eleven Day Government

**The revolutionary government that Kras Mazov headed in Graad during the Turn-of-the-Century Revolution.**

Encyclopedia brings it up whenever Mazov does: the economist and historian, father of scientific communism, was a leading figure on the Graad side of that revolution (also called the Centennial Revolution) and led this government.

See also: [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov), [Communism](#communism)

### Filippe III

*Also: Filippe III the Squanderer, Filippe III, the Squanderer, Filippe the III, Filippe the Third, Fillipe III, King Filippe III, Old Filippe, Squanderer, the Squanderer*

**An extravagant old king of Revachol nicknamed the Squanderer; his bronze statue on horseback towers over the roundabout near the Whirling-in-Rags.**

The plaque on the horseback monument calls him the greatest of the Filippian kings of Revachol, son of Filippe II the Opulent and father of Filippe IV the Insane. He was famous for wasting the national treasury on gold, ceremonial weapons and monuments to himself, and for his love of the purest cocaine; René Arnoux and Gaston Martin can tell you all about it, and treasure hunters still dream of finding his stash.

René sees the statue as a reminder of what Revachol once was. Not everyone agrees: some call Filippe III a syphilitic murderer, though Encyclopedia insists he wasn't syphilitic, just mad.

See also: [Filippian kings](#filippian-kings), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Revachol](#revachol), [Royalists](#royalists), [Sol Aurum](#sol-aurum), [Suzerain](#suzerain), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Deserter, an old communard, despises him as a syphilitic murderer (Encyclopedia says he was not syphilitic, just mad), and the bullet hole in the statue's heart turns out to be his work.

</details>

### Filippian kings

*Also: Filippian Kings of Revachol, Filippian, Filippian cocaine*

**Revachol's line of kings named Filippe, remembered for gold, grand monuments and royal cocaine, which was purple and said to be twice as strong.**

René Arnoux claims the Filippian kings used the purest cocaine for clarity of vision and calls their age 'Regnum Cocainum', Revachol's finest years. Shivers recalls the mausoleums where they were buried with their doctors and admirals.

According to an old communard veteran, the communist Party blew up every one of the 141 Filippian monuments. The ruined sea fort off the Martinaise coast was built by Filippe II.

See also: [Filippe III](#filippe-iii), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revachol](#revachol), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

### Franconegro

*Also: Franconegros, Franconigerian, His Innocence Franconegro, Innocence Franconegro, Franconigerian cavalry, Franconigerian knights*

**An Innocence crowned about 500 years ago: a militarist and nationalist ruler whom many, especially the Mesque, revere as the father of nations.**

Franconegro built concrete churches and cathedrals and tried to settle the conflict between aristocracy and bourgeoisie with a unified society in which everyone had a place, yet could rise to nobility through virtue. His famously wide heavy cavalry swept over the nations of his enemies in the fifth century, bringing a unified currency in its wake. Bird's Nest Roy sells a set of Franconigerian cavalry figurines, and Kim admits he used to be very serious about those knights.

The faces of innocences appear on banknotes, so Savoir Faire calls cash 'Franconegros and Solas'. The Paledriver claims to remember his coronation on the grand stairs of Raehl.

See also: [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Fascism](#fascism), [Innocence](#innocence), [reál](#reál)

### Frissel

*Also: Frissel the First, King Frissel, King Frissel the First*

**Revachol's king during the communist Revolution; when his uncle King Guillaume fled, Frissel was left behind and publicly executed.**

René Arnoux served him as a Royal Carabineer: the medals on René's chest were awarded in Frissel's service, and he remembers how Frissel sent his cousin Drysant to crush the unrest in one decisive strike. Even René calls him a 'damn clown', and Gaston Martin jokes about 'Frissel the Fun'.

To the communards he embodied everything they wanted to overthrow; the radical journal La Fumée puts a satirical portrait of him, sprouting white antlers, on its cover.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune)

### Ikeira Operation

**A seven-year military campaign in which King Guillaume's army forcibly brought the Ikeira tribes of Le Petit Continent under Revacholian rule.**

It took place in the southeastern part of Le Petit Continent, back when Revachol was ruled by its kings. René Arnoux served in it as a young man and came home at 22 to find his sweetheart in the arms of another man.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revachol](#revachol)

### Insulindian Citizens Militia

*Also: ICM, Black and White Army of the Revolution*

**The ICM: the official name of the communards' army during the Revolution. The RCM may be descended from it.**

You may spot the initials on an old emblem. Kim calls it the Black and White Army of the Revolution and says hardly anyone remembers it now; he and Encyclopedia both think the RCM may descend from it.

See also: [Commune](#commune), [Revolution](#revolution), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Deserter, Iosef Lilianovich Dros, is a last holdover of the ICM, recruited in Jamrock in '07.

</details>

### Irene La Navigateur

*Also: Queen of Suresne*

**A queen of Suresne (forerunner of Sur-la-Clef) who sent expedition after expedition into the pale, which led to the discovery of the 'New New World'.**

Joyce Messier tells how the pale was once thought impossible to cross. The queen sent eight expeditions into it, one after another: five crews never came back and two returned having lost their minds.

According to Encyclopedia, her principal advisor was Dolores Dei, on whose advice the costly voyages were made. They were vindicated by the discovery of the land you are standing on.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Revachol](#revachol), [Insulinde](#insulinde)

### Operation Death Blow

*Also: Death Blow, the Landing, The Landing, Landing, Operation Death Blow, Coalition landings*

**The Coalition's military code name for its '08 assault that retook Revachol from the Commune. Locals simply call it the Landing.**

According to René Arnoux, Martinaise was one of three footholds for the attack, along with Stella Maris and the Delta. Airships filled the sky and poured soldiers into the city, and the old sea fort off the Martinaise coast was wrecked in the fighting.

The Landing brought down the Commune and opened the era of Coalition rule over Revachol.

See also: [Commune](#commune), [Coalition](#coalition), [Revolution](#revolution), [Martinaise](#martinaise)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

An old communard you may meet very late in the case is still bitter about the name: he asks who would call an operation against a population of millions 'Death Blow'.

</details>

### People's Pile

**A primitive nuclear reactor built to bring poor communities cheap electricity; it failed on start-up and leaked radioactive waste into the River Esperance.**

Encyclopedia calls it a Type U particle decay generator ('pile' is another word for such a reactor) that broke down immediately after entering service. Bird's Nest Roy at the pawnshop says he was with the Emergency Relief Brigade that cleaned up after the disaster, and has had to take Pyrholidon for radiation sickness ever since. According to Cindy, veterans of the clean-up sell their stash of pills in Porta Rosa.

*Real-world inspiration:* Real nuclear disasters such as Chernobyl; early nuclear reactors were called 'piles' (as in Chicago Pile-1).

See also: [Porta Rosa](#porta-rosa)

### Perikarnassis

*Also: Perikarnassians, Perikarnassian*

**An ancient civilisation of Elysium, thousands of years old, whose cities, religion and ideas still echo through history.**

It comes up in passing: its great marble cities, its serfs, an eight-eyed imperial eagle, and a proto-Perikarnassian script from which the letter A is said to descend. The Perikarnassians studied the pale some 6,000 years ago and called it the Western Plain, and their theory that love is the key to immortality is still quoted. The Perikarnassian Church and its Star of Perikarnassis survive to this day.

The Coalition Warship Archer counts nearly 8,000 years of recorded history since the 'Perikarnassian Incident'.

*Real-world inspiration:* Classical antiquity; the name echoes the ancient Greek city of Halicarnassus.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Innocence](#innocence)

### Ramout Karzai

**An ancient hero of Graad; your skills invoke him as an image of primal, war-painted strength.**

When you smear your hands with coal at the furnace in the Doomed Commercial Area, Inland Empire feels his spirit pulsing through you and suggests war-paint. Pain Threshold compares Titus Hardie to him after a bone-jarring fist bump: Titus is stronger than he looks, and he looks like Ramout Karzai.

See also: [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Pain Threshold](#pain-threshold)

### Revolution

*Also: the Revolution, the revolution, Antecentennial Revolution, Turn-of-the-Century Revolution, Turn-Of-The-Century Revolution, Centennial Revolution, turn of the century revolutions*

**The communist uprising in Revachol, about fifty years before the game. It overthrew the monarchy.**

Communist revolutionaries took the city from its royal government and founded the Commune. Their victory did not last: the Coalition of foreign nations invaded and crushed it. The Revolution is still a living memory for older characters, and the scars (bullet holes, ruins, grudges) are everywhere.

The game also calls it the Antecentennial or Turn-of-the-Century Revolution. It was not limited to Revachol: in Graad, Kras Mazov headed a revolutionary government of his own.

*Real-world inspiration:* The Paris Commune of 1871 and the revolutions of the 20th century.

See also: [Commune](#commune), [Coalition](#coalition), [Communism](#communism), [Royalists](#royalists)

### Royal Carabineers

*Also: Royal Carabineer, carabineer, carabineers*

**The royal army unit René Arnoux served in during the Revolution; the old veteran still wears its colourful uniform.**

René says he served the Carabineers under King Frissel the First, Guillaume 'Le Lion' and, before them, King Filippe the Fifth. He insists they were the last ones keeping the war's worst killers in check, and that without them Revachol might not be standing at all. He blames 782 Carabineer deaths on the incompetence of the royal commander Drysant.

Gaston Martin says René's Carabineer pension is not enough to live on, so Evrart Claire created a night watchman job for him.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Royalists](#royalists), [Revolution](#revolution), [Setting Sun](#setting-sun), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

You can end up wearing an old Carabineer uniform yourself; Kim suggests you take up historical re-enactment.

</details>

### Royalists

*Also: royalist, kingsman, kingsmen*

**Supporters of Revachol's old monarchy, which the communist revolution overthrew.**

Some old veterans, like René Arnoux of the Royal Carabineers, fought for the king against the revolutionaries and still hold onto that loyalty.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Revolution](#revolution), [Reactionary](#reactionary)

### Setting Sun

*Also: Setting Sun medal*

**A sun-shaped medal for seasoned combat veterans of King Frissel the First's army; René Arnoux wears one on his chest.**

Encyclopedia explains the decoration when you ask René about his medals. It hangs next to his Croix de Bravoure. Gaston Martin notes that you don't get one just for a service record: you have to get shot, repeatedly.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Royal Carabineers](#royal-carabineers), [Royalists](#royalists), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If you win a red check by impressing René with a heroic story of your own, he gives you his Setting Sun. You can later pin it on the chest of the ageing woman in police gear who shouts through a megaphone on the boardwalk.

</details>

### Sol Aurum

**King Filippe III's legendary treasure chamber, crammed with gold and riches; a byword for royal excess.**

Encyclopedia says the king turned his bedroom into a vault of gold bars, coins, ornate weapons and chalices, and rumour had it that he slept on a heap of gold-dipped feathers.

Joyce Messier recalls that it was eventually melted down for its gold and platinum and sold to buy bullets.

See also: [Filippe III](#filippe-iii), [Suzerain](#suzerain)

### Suzerain

*Also: Revachol the Suzerain, Suzerain of Revachol, Suzerainty*

**The title of Revachol's old kings, and the name for the royal era when Revachol was a rich colonial superpower; the communist Revolution swept it away.**

As René puts it, the suzerain is the king. Under the Suzerain, Revachol ruled vassals and colonies across the world (Siigay, the 'Apricot Suzerainty', and Tioumoutiri on Iilmaraa among them), and the king granted royal monopolies to trading companies such as Wild Pines. Its old flag still hangs on the odd wall in Martinaise.

Joyce counts today's Zone of Control as the third Revachol, after the Suzerain and the Commune. In real English, a suzerain is a ruler with power over vassal states.

See also: [Coalition](#coalition), [Commune](#commune), [Filippe III](#filippe-iii), [Revolution](#revolution), [Royalists](#royalists), [Siigay](#siigay), [Suzerainty: The Board Game](#suzerainty-the-board-game)

### The Perikarnassian

**The first innocence in history: an ancient, mysterious figure said to have invented god and the idea that all people are equal before god.**

Almost nothing certain is known about the Perikarnassian. Even the gender is unclear, though later tradition assumed a man, and the innocence Franconegro called him Pius. Icons show a young man with molten gold pouring from his mouth, because everything he said was gold. He is also credited with introducing the gold standard.

As the first innocence, he declared that there should be more like him. His disciples are thought to have formed the Holy Party, known today as the Founding Party, which still searches for new innocences.

See also: [Innocence](#innocence), [Perikarnassian Church](#perikarnassian-church), [The Greatest Innocence](#the-greatest-innocence)

### World Revolution

**The worldwide wave of communist revolution about fifty years ago, which included Revachol's own. It failed, and Revachol ended up under foreign occupation.**

Encyclopedia calls Revachol the disgraced former capital of the world, half a century after the failed world revolution. Joyce Messier puts the toll at around 40 million people shot, and the communists themselves all got shot.

It had three anthems: La Revacholière in Revachol, 'Brave Children, Favourites of History' in Graad, and a third from Hsin-Yao. Almost nobody remembers that its third outbreak, in the Safre Empire, was put down in '06.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune), [Communism](#communism), [World Republic](#world-republic), [Zone of Control](#zone-of-control)

### Zygismunt the Great

*Also: Zygismunt*

**An ancient Zsiemsk ruler whose zaffer blue and white banners live on as the classic colours of the Stas-Rajko motor carriage.**

Encyclopedia remembers this much while you admire a Stas-Rajko in Jamrock, and not much else about ancient Zsiemsk kings.

See also: [Zsiemsk](#zsiemsk), [Motor carriage](#motor-carriage)

## Culture & Media

### An Asshole Is A Mouth For Shit (And I'm Puking)

*Also: An Asshole Is A Mouth For Shit, Asshole Is A Mouth For Shit*

**DJ Flacio's crude debut single, freshly premiered on the Speedfreaks FM radio station; DJ Mesh is sure it is the future of music.**

DJ Mesh calls its sound 'stinkcore' and says listeners keep phoning in to request it. You can ask him about it by having the dispatcher patch you through to the station from the radio in Kim's motor carriage.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Lilienne's twins in the fishing village can recite the single's on-air introduction, even though they claim they don't know what a radio is and simply 'heard it in their heads'. Calling DJ Mesh confirms that the track really did debut a few days ago.

</details>

### Anodic music

*Also: anodic dance music, anodic*

**A new, hard style of electronic dance music; the speedfreaks by the old church dream of turning the building into a club for it.**

As Acele puts it: anodic, cathodic, music made with electronic instruments. It is meant to be harder than ordinary dance music, mostly skips traditional vocals (thought of as rock, and a bit backward), and is played at places like The Paliseum. Egg Head has been blasting the same Arno van Eyck track for a month.

You can joke that you refuse to become an 'Anodic Cop' on top of all your other copotypes.

*Real-world inspiration:* Techno and other electronic dance music.

See also: [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Copotype](#copotype)

### Antistar

*Also: The Antistar*

**A notorious Occidental rock star famous for extreme drug use; a slim biography of him sits on the bookstore's biography shelf.**

He is said to have shot morphine into one eyeball and cocaine into the other. Encyclopedia describes him as a Vespertine rock and roll star who took so many drugs that he eventually mutated into a corpse. Klaasje uses him as the yardstick for a truly huge fortune.

See also: [Klaasje](#klaasje)

### Arno van Eyck

*Also: van Eyck, Van Eyck*

**A celebrated pioneer of anodic music (new synthesizer-driven dance music), probably Oranjese. The young ravers you meet idolise him.**

Egg Head has been pumping one Arno van Eyck jam for a month and plans to keep it going all year; Andre says it is really a reworking of a Pepi Popikarnassos track. Acele once saw van Eyck at the Paliseum, a club and synthesizer workshop on Boogie Street in Jamrock, and two youths on the plaza call Cindy the SKULL a true artist of the future, just like him. Encyclopedia guesses from his name that he's from Oranje.

Helping Egg Head make that jam hit harder is one of the side tasks you can take on.

See also: [Oranje](#oranje), [Jamrock](#jamrock)

### Automatik Polychrome

**Apparently a colour film process from the days of Twenties movie stars; Electrochemistry pictures Klaasje glowing on screen 'in Automatik Polychrome'.**

The name only comes up in passing, when Electrochemistry gushes over Klaasje's film-star looks and remembers some old movie posters.

*Real-world inspiration:* Technicolor

See also: [Klaasje](#klaasje), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

### boiadeiro

*Also: boiadeiros, boia*

**A cow herder of the Mesque steppes and a romantic, lone-rider ideal of cool; Mañana at the harbour gates lives by its code.**

Your Encyclopedia explains that a boiadeiro ('boia' for short) is a herdsman from upstream Magritte, the great steppes of northern Mesque. The figure has become a pop-culture icon, with boiadeiro films, boots and swagger, and a code of conduct reaching back to Franconigerian times.

Call Me Mañana, the man at the harbour gates, follows this code: he avoids work, rides alone and calls people 'boiadeiro' or 'boia' as a term of respect. Several characters wear boiadeiro boots.

*Real-world inspiration:* 'Boiadeiro' is Portuguese for a cattle drover; the figure plays the role of the cowboy or gaucho of Western films.

See also: [Egg Head](#egg-head)

### Charlie Spillane

*Also: Spillane*

**A character in the detective novel 'Dick Mullen and the Mistaken Identity': Mullen's old friend, whose murder sets the plot in motion.**

On a cold night Spillane asks Mullen to drive him from Vesper to a small town on the Insulindian coast. He is then shot dead in a bar there by a man matching Mullen's description, and Mullen is arrested for the murder.

As Mullen digs into the case, he learns that Spillane was mixed up in a local amphetamine-smuggling ring. That gives a corrupt police captain, a powerful politician's son and the art collector Torvald all reasons to want him dead.

See also: [Deanna Deneuve](#deanna-deneuve)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The last fifth of your copy of the book has fallen out, so you never learn who the killer is. You have to pick one yourself.

</details>

### Cocaine Skull

*Also: the Cocaine Skull*

**A Martinaise urban legend about a famous designer buried with a quarter kilo of cocaine still lodged in his sinuses; treasure hunters dig for it.**

According to the story told by one of the drinkers in the fishing village, a celebrated lead designer born in Martinaise died at his desk working on an advert so minimal it was pure white. The autopsy supposedly found 250 grams of pure cocaine packed into his nose, and he was buried with it somewhere around Martinaise: under a pipe on the shore, further down the coast, in a hidden mausoleum or on the islets in the bay, depending on who you ask.

Archaeologists, gangsters, junkies and ad-agency people have all mounted expeditions to find it. Kim points out that sinuses cannot hold that much of anything; Electrochemistry is very interested anyway.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

### Codex Urik

**A Wirrâl sourcebook about the dweorgr, the fantasy game's hairy, hard-drinking, axe-swinging folk; it sits in the bookstore's pile of board games.**

Its full title is 'Codex Urik: the Industrious Dweorgr of Wirrâl', a guide to creating dweorg characters and their culture. Wirrâl is a popular sword-and-sorcery board game, and the shop is full of its merchandise. Leafing through it invites some unflattering comparisons between you and the bulbous-nosed dweorg on the cover.

*Real-world inspiration:* Fantasy role-playing game supplements such as Dungeons & Dragons race books (the dweorgr are this world's dwarves).

See also: [Annette](#annette)

### Contact Mike

**A boxing champion who rose from the slums of Saint-Batiste to the top of the sport despite serious brain trauma; your mangled brain idolises him.**

He comes to mind when Acele mentions her contact microphone, and Harry insists on holding him up to her as an inspirational role model. Talking Acele into being 'more like Contact Mike' gives you a Thought named after him, and with it you can even dedicate a karaoke song to him.

See also: [Thought Cabinet](#thought-cabinet), [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Your old colleagues at Precinct 41 know all about your Contact Mike obsession (to you he is a 5,000-to-1 outsider who made it); Jean Vicquemare hates the guy.

</details>

### Deanna Deneuve

*Also: Deneuve*

**A character in the novel 'Dick Mullen and the Mistaken Identity': a beautiful, mysterious woman Mullen meets at the bar where his friend was killed.**

She tells Mullen she was Charlie Spillane's lover and that he was involved in amphetamine smuggling. Later Mullen discovers that the art collector Torvald was once involved with her too.

See also: [Charlie Spillane](#charlie-spillane)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

She and Mullen become lovers, and soon after he wakes up to find her dead beside him. Your copy of the book is missing its ending, so you have to guess who killed her.

</details>

### Dick Mullen

*Also: Dick Mullen and the Murder in the Orchard, Dick Mullen on the Job, Get Me Mullen!, Mullen, Richard P. Mullen, The Sordid Affair of Dick Mullen, The Stalwart Adventures of Richard P. Mullen*

**The hero of a popular series of pulp detective novels: a tough, brilliant Vespertine police detective who always cracks the case.**

The bookstore in Martinaise has whole shelves of Mullen titles, from his classic hard-boiled phase onward. Annette, the little girl outside the bookstore, reads them and will happily tell you that you look nothing like him.

You can find a copy of 'Dick Mullen and the Mistaken Identity' with its ending torn out, leaving you to work out the culprit yourself. Encyclopedia is keen to remind you that Mullen is fictional, and that he is not you.

See also: [Annette](#annette), [Dick Mullen and the Mistaken Identity](#dick-mullen-and-the-mistaken-identity)

### DJ Flacio

*Also: Flacio*

**One of the two shouting DJs of Speedfreaks FM, a wild Revachol radio station; his debut single has just come out.**

He co-hosts with DJ Mesh, mixing loud rock with the sounds of car crashes and illegal street races. You may catch the station on the radio in Kim's motor carriage. Lilienne's twins in the fishing village somehow know all about his new single, which sets off a small mystery.

See also: [DJ Mesh](#dj-mesh), [Kineema](#kineema), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

### DJ Mesh

**The loud co-host of Speedfreaks FM, a radio station that calls itself Revachol's hottest; you can get him on the line through the police radio.**

Mesh hosts alongside DJ Flacio and calls himself a 'spoken word guy'. Through Precinct 57's radio officer you can call him to ask about the station's broadcasts; he finds a cop calling him hilarious, and turns hostile if you push the wrong politics on him.

See also: [DJ Flacio](#dj-flacio), [Alice](#alice), [Kineema](#kineema)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Kim's own motor carriage radio has Speedfreaks FM saved as a favourite, something he would rather you did not bring up.

</details>

### Gabriel Buenguerro

**A dashing Mesque film star of the Revolution era, famous for boiadeiro (cowboy-style) pictures; the Paledriver is still devoted to him.**

According to the Paledriver he was the biggest star of his day: girls fainted in the cinema aisles and schoolboys memorised his lines. She keeps his photograph in a lavish amber frame, compares your jawline unfavourably with his, and mentions films such as 'Segure-me, Paraíso', 'O Céu Dourado' and 'Pergunte à Poeira'.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution)

### Gareth Morrand

*Also: Mr. Morrand*

**A former cop who now hosts a crime show on Channel 8 radio; a confused old woman on the boardwalk keeps calling out for 'Mr. Morrand'.**

Encyclopedia identifies him as the ex-cop turned radio host, and Esprit de Corps adds that he worked in Traffic. The Hardie Boys can't stand him: Alain calls him a racist, and Titus says nobody trusts him.

See also: [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The old woman who calls herself the Pigs spends her lonely days with nothing but his voice on Channel 8 for company.

</details>

### Headless FALN Rider

*Also: FALN Rider, Headless Rider*

**A Martinaise urban legend: a headless man in a FALN tracksuit who rides the streets by night, searching for the FALN cap he lost with his head.**

Idiot Doom Spiral in the Fishing Village loves telling the full tale, with its many conflicting versions: some say the rider was an undercover cop beheaded by a gang, others a jockey who met a clothes line. He rides a horse, or a bull, or a pig, depending on who you ask, and is said to appear near the canal at midnight. Kim insists he is just a story.

Bird's Nest Roy's pawnshop sells a small figurine of the rider, complete with a tiny cap, which Roy says brings luck.

*Real-world inspiration:* The Headless Horseman of Washington Irving's 'The Legend of Sleepy Hollow'.

See also: [FALN](#faln), [Idiot Doom Spiral](#idiot-doom-spiral)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If you own the figurine, you can offer it as a gift to the woman you lost, in a dream late in the game.

</details>

### High Speed Love

*Also: High Speed Love: The Tragic True Story of Jacob Irw and Alfie Deletraz*

**A biography in the Martinaise bookstore about the tragic romance between racing drivers Jacob Irw and Alfie Deletraz.**

Written by one Cecilia Averbrook, the book sits on the bookstore's biography shelf with Irw's blond mane on the cover. It tells how two of the finest TipTop Tournée racers fell in love, and how Irw carried on after Alfie's death.

See also: [Jacob Irw](#jacob-irw), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

### Innocence of Capital

**A multi-volume work of communist theory by Puncher and Wattmann; the student communists have just finished its second volume.**

According to the Echo Maker, the book argues that communists must possess as little capital as possible to keep their thinking undiluted by it. Steban lists it first when you ask what his reading group reads.

See also: [Puncher and Wattmann](#puncher-and-wattmann), [Communism](#communism)

### Jacob Irw

*Also: Irw*

**A legendary, death-defying TipTop Tournée racing champion; Harry keeps blaming him for the reckless driving he finds evidence of around Martinaise.**

Irw was famous for driving like a man who wanted to die, especially after his fellow racer and great love Alfie Deletraz was killed at Fjordhammer; death simply refused to take him. Their story is told in the biography 'High Speed Love' in the Martinaise bookstore.

When you find signs of a crazed motor carriage driver in the district, you can insist to Kim that Irw must be behind it. Kim is not convinced.

See also: [High Speed Love](#high-speed-love), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Motor carriage](#motor-carriage)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Irw has nothing to do with it. The wrecked motor carriage in the sea is your own police vehicle, which you drove into the ocean yourself, and Jean Vicquemare is not amused by the Irw story.

</details>

### La Revacholière

**A revolutionary soldiers' marching song; the name is also used for the city of Revachol herself, imagined as a living being.**

An old man you may meet calls it real music, real proletkult, a 'chanson de soldat', unlike your rock and roll. You can say you know it as the marching song of the World Revolution.

He also recalls frightened children during the shelling who began talking to the city itself, begging it to protect them and calling it La Revacholière.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Commune](#commune), [Revachol](#revachol), [Shivers](#shivers)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Late in the game, through Shivers, you may hear the city speak to you directly, and Kim asks what La Revacholière told you about the future.

</details>

### Lo Manthang stick fighting

*Also: stick fighting*

**A martial art from the isle of Lo Manthang, fought with slender wooden sticks. Trant Heidelstam has practised it for nearly twenty years.**

Trant, a talkative trivia lover you can meet near the FELD building, will happily explain it, along with the history of stick fighting in many other cultures, at great length. He compares his hobby to a pyrholidon habit.

### Loos, Radio City

*Also: Radio City, Loos, Radio City '87*

**A science fiction novel; its new release, 'Loos, Radio City '87', comes up when you phone the Jamrock library about a library card.**

When you phone the Central Jamrock Public Library about a soggy library card, the librarian mentions that the card holder's husband asked for this new sci-fi release, which the library doesn't have yet. Logic immediately remembers the tired working class woman reading the very same book at the bookstand on the plaza.

See also: [Martinaise](#martinaise)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The working class woman is Billie Méjean, the card's owner, and the husband who wanted the book is the dead man found on the boardwalk.

</details>

### Lord Wrôthgär

*Also: Wrôthgär*

**A powerful old southern king in the pulp fantasy book Hjelmdallermann: the Man from Hjelmdall.**

In the story he begs the Man from Hjelmdall to rid his kingdom of the Nachtherrer, a clan of blind wizards, and the hero makes him pay a high price for the job.

See also: [Nachtherrer](#nachtherrer)

### Lu Jiatun

**A Seraise poet from the previous century whose poem cycle features 'Girl Child Communism', a ghostly figure who keeps returning.**

Encyclopedia confirms that Lu Jiatun composed the work in the Fifties of the last century. Trant Heidelstam calls it the 'Mirova Cycle' (or 'Mirova 82').

See also: [Mirova](#mirova), [Communism](#communism)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

When the big graffiti in Martinaise comes up, Trant may recognise its words as a line from this poem cycle.

</details>

### Man from Hjelmdall

*Also: Hjelmdallermann, Hjelmdallermenn, Hjelmdall Saga, Man from Hjelmdall Series*

**A hugely popular series of violent barbarian adventure novels, sold by the hundred at the Martinaise bookstore.**

The hero is a giant, muscular warrior from the frozen Northlands who wields two great swords, Sturm and Drang, and travels from land to land cutting down wizards, monsters and armies, often with his blood-brother Tyrbald at his side.

Plaisance, who runs the bookstore, finds the books immoral and violent but admits they sell incredibly well, and Kim calls the series 'endearingly racist'. The books have fans around Martinaise, and the hero even appears on a T-shirt in the pawnshop. You can buy two of the books to read.

*Real-world inspiration:* Conan the Barbarian and similar sword-and-sorcery pulp fiction.

See also: [Hjelmdallermann: the Man from Hjelmdall](#hjelmdallermann-the-man-from-hjelmdall), [Man from Hjelmdall and the Devil Woman](#man-from-hjelmdall-and-the-devil-woman), [Plaisance](#plaisance)

### Miss Page-Three

**Electrochemistry's name for a freckled pin-up girl printed on 'page three', who it says will lead you to the origin of disco.**

Electrochemistry urges you to 'party' with her right there in public, which Rhetoric calls gross public indecency. Inland Empire senses that the freckles only make you sad.

*Real-world inspiration:* The 'Page 3 girl' pin-ups of British tabloid newspapers.

See also: [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry), [Copotype](#copotype)

### Nachtherrer

**The villains of the pulp fantasy book Hjelmdallermann: a clan of blind underground wizards who traded their souls for demonic powers.**

The Man from Hjelmdall and his blood-brother Tyrbald are hired by Lord Wrôthgär to destroy them, and fight their way through hordes of 'orkenoid' thralls to reach them.

See also: [Lord Wrôthgär](#lord-wrôthgär)

### No Truce with the Furies

*Also: Furies, The Furies Are at Home in the Mirror*

**A dramatic, poetic name your detective can suggest more than once, for the murder case in Kim's notebook and later for a dance club.**

When Kim asks what to call the case, one of your options is THE FURIES ARE AT HOME IN THE MIRROR. Kim isn't keen: he says he isn't feeling the inner strife the name refers to and would prefer something less poetic.

*Real-world inspiration:* Disco Elysium's own original working title, 'No Truce With The Furies'.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If Andre's crew get their club running in the old church, you can suggest calling it No Truce with the Furies, and Acele may make it official. If you suggested 'Furies' for the case, a later glimpse of Kim's notebook shows he used it after all.

</details>

### Oranjese Map of the Waterways

*Also: Map of the Waterways*

**An old Oranjese sailor's tattoo: a web of lines with stars marking the port cities the wearer has sailed to.**

Joyce Messier explains that sailors of the Dolorian century, more than 300 years ago, tattooed their bodies to map their travels across the oceans. The custom has since changed into a style of mercenary tattoos in the Occident.

See also: [Photo of Tattoos](#photo-of-tattoos), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Oranje](#oranje), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The hanged man's chest is covered in one, which points to his mercenary past.

</details>

### Ostentatious Orchestrations

**A disco rock band; Klaasje says their songs led the loud disco music she heard coming from your room during your binge.**

Encyclopedia winces at the name and would rather say as little as possible about them, adding that they are more of a disco rock band anyway. In Revachol, disco really meant one man: Guillaume Le Million.

See also: [Klaasje](#klaasje), [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia)

### Paradox B

**A fringe science magazine from Graad about theories like telekinesis and pale appearing inside the isolas; skeptics treat it as crackpot reading.**

Encyclopedia says its mission is to explain such theories before they get out of hand. The popular radio show 'Truth Hunt' is its broadcast counterpart, and Gary, the cryptofascist in Martinaise, admits most of what he says comes from one or the other.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology), [Cryptid](#cryptid)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

At the end of the case, Jean Vicquemare dismisses Harry's stranger findings as Paradox B material.

</details>

### Project Dread Board

**An old green chalkboard in the abandoned studio of Fortress Accident, a failed radio game company, covered in concept art and a doomed production schedule.**

The board belonged to the team behind 'Wirrâl Untethered', a call-in radio game set in a frozen world drifting away from its sun. It holds photos of icy wastelands, slogans such as 'Call in, tune out!' and 'Heat death of the Universe', and countless drawings of welkins: lithe, pointy-eared fantasy creatures, including a sinister 'high welkin' who wants every other race purged.

A production calendar from the year '50 fills up with tasks and then empties out towards the end, a silent record of the studio running out of time and money. Kim is struck by how much effort went into it.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Soona](#soona), [Radiocomputer](#radiocomputer)

### Queen Lydiaana

*Also: Lydiaana*

**The seductive, scheming queen of Cloud City in the pulp novel 'Man from Hjelmdall and the Devil Woman'.**

In the book, the barbarian hero is captured by a tribe of female warriors and brought to her throne room. She laughs off his vows of purity and tries to win him over with potions and spells: first he is to please her, then lead her armies against the cannibals.

You can read the book after buying it from the bookstore in the Doomed Commercial Area.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

By the final pages Lydiaana is dead, and the hero rides off on his gryffin while cannibals storm the gates.

</details>

### Reál and Reality

**A book of communist theory that the student communists' reading group has worked through; its title is a pun on the reál, the currency.**

Steban lists it among the texts the group has read, just before Wertmüller's 'The Mega-Structure of History'. Conceptualization remarks that communist theorists love puns. The Echo Maker grumbles that the new translation is garbage.

See also: [Puncher and Wattmann](#puncher-and-wattmann), [reál](#reál), [Communism](#communism)

### Rietveldt

**An anodic music pioneer; the punks on the plaza name him alongside Arno van Eyck as a true artist of the future.**

Fuck the World uses him as the highest praise for his friend Cindy the SKULL. Noid brings him up too, as one of the famous people nobody accuses of mass murder.

### Sad FM

**A radio station that plays sad, slow rock songs; your detective seems to know its frequency by heart.**

Inland Empire can't resist urging you to tune in, and Kim agrees to 'blast' it with a resigned shrug.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

You can play it on the motor skiff on the way to the island. The old man you meet there hates the station, calling its songs counter-revolutionary dirges.

</details>

### Sam Bo

*Also: Sam Bo style, Sam Bo-style, Samaran boxing*

**Samaran boxing: a graceful, violent one-on-one fighting style from the Samaran isola; 'Sam Bo style' means stealth, cleverness and cool.**

It once covered a wide range of martial disciplines, from archery to mounted combat, but today it mostly means elegant single combat. Your skills use the phrase whenever you pull off something slick, such as trying to slip away from Garte without settling your debt; Garte is sarcastically unimpressed.

Klaasje admits she is no trained Sam Bo fighter.

*Real-world inspiration:* The name echoes sambo, a Soviet martial art.

See also: [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia), [Garte](#garte), [Klaasje](#klaasje), [People's Republic of Samara](#peoples-republic-of-samara), [Samara](#samara)

### Samir Loisir

**A legendary Gurdi businessman who rose from the coal mines to fabulous wealth; Savoir Faire holds him up as proof that anyone can get rich.**

Encyclopedia says he made his first millions selling the ultra-rare mineral phasium to the luxury jeweller Zachaël et Cie, and the rest through wise investments and the invention of pleochroic lights.

Savoir Faire claims he grew up eating dirt in Gurdi's mines and still made fifty-five billion reál, so you have no excuses. Logic thinks both the fortune and the dirt-eating are exaggerated.

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Savoir Faire](#savoir-faire), [reál](#reál)

### Smallest Church in Saint-Saëns

*Also: The Smallest Church in Saint-Saëns, The Smallest Church In Saint-Saëns*

**A slow, melancholy organ song about a tiny church by the sea; you can find it on tape and sing it at the Whirling-in-Rags karaoke.**

The lyrics describe a tiny church in Saint-Saëns with an even tinier yard, the sea mist and pale cliffs, and they end on a note of loneliness. Garte recognises the song at once.

A tape of it turns up in an abandoned summer house on the coast. You need a tape player or porta-reel to listen to it, and you can hand it to Garte if you want to perform it on the karaoke stage.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Garte](#garte), [Klaasje](#klaasje)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It is the sad song you sang during your binge; Klaasje and Sylvie, the Whirling's former waitress, can both identify it. Egg Head turns it down as far too sad for his dance music.

</details>

### Son of Lung

*Also: Far Out Son of Lung*

**A police urban legend about a detective so far undercover, or so far gone, that he forgets who he is. People compare you to him.**

Kim calls it an urban myth about an officer who goes so deep undercover he can't remember his own identity. Another explanation is that it's a cultural reference to a detective who uses wildly experimental methods.

A heckler in the Whirling-in-Rags mockingly calls you the 'Far Out Son of Lung': a supercop who drinks himself into delirium to solve crimes.

*Real-world inspiration:* The name echoes 'Far-Out Son of Lung and the Ramblings of a Madman', a 1995 track by the electronic group The Future Sound of London.

See also: [Amnesia](#amnesia)

### Speedfreaks FM

**A dance-music radio station hosted by DJ Mesh, which turns up in a strange radio mystery on the coast.**

It is saved as the favourite station on the radio of Kim's motor carriage, though he would rather not talk about it.

Two boys in the fishing village seem to have heard Speedfreaks FM without any radio, which sets off a small side mystery.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [speedfreak](#speedfreak), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

### Stained Glass Window

*Also: Shards of Dei*

**A cracked stained glass window in the abandoned Dolorian church showing Dolores Dei, the 'young mother of humanism', in apricot-coloured light.**

The tall, sad figure is painted on a single pane, with a crack running across her body; cold air and snow get in through it. Visual Calculus can reconstruct the shattered part: once there was an older woman below her and a line of text. You can kneel before her, and Kim quietly draws an x-shaped cross from shoulder to shoulder.

Kim identifies the building as the Dolorian Church of Humanity, also called the Small Pinewood Church, the last of seven old stave churches on the coast.

See also: [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Dolorian](#dolorian), [Visual Calculus](#visual-calculus)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Kim suspects the church was shot up in a police raid some time ago, and that Precinct 41 was among the three precincts involved.

</details>

### Stormers

**A local sports team that keeps losing; Titus Hardie thinks they can't play at all.**

In an Esprit de Corps vision, a policeman checks the box scores and sees they lost again, 17-32. Kim jokes that he would have to grow ten centimetres to play flanker for them.

See also: [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### The Glinting Curve

*Also: Glinting Curve*

**A book by M. Thibault, the last title listed on the soggy library card found on a dead man on the boardwalk.**
