The card's owner was clearly a keen reader: the list also includes 'Radiothriller' and 'Stand a Little Less Between Me and the Sun', and a library stamp shows The Glinting Curve was returned. The title helps you work out whose card it is.

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Working Class Woman in the apartment building recognises it as her own book; her husband had gone to return it to the library.

</details>

### TipTop Tournée

*Also: TipTop*

**A hugely popular interisolary motor racing series, famous for its speed, its absurd sponsorship deals and its many deaths; Kim is a fan.**

In Revachol the Tournée is held every four years at the Zéro Carrousel racing circuit. Kim, who adores his own motor carriage, perks up if you say you like TipTop, and Harry can mock his outfit as that of a 'washed-up TipTop racer'.

The bookstore sells 'High Speed Love', a biography of the madcap champion driver Jacob Irw. The communist journal La Fumée runs a long essay condemning the races as a capitalist ritual of brands and destruction.

*Real-world inspiration:* Formula One motor racing, especially its dangerous early decades.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Kineema](#kineema), [Motor carriage](#motor-carriage), [La Fumée](#la-fumée)

### Truth Hunt

**A popular radio show about cryptids, conspiracy theories and fringe history. Gary the Cryptofascist gets his ideas from it.**

Each week its two eccentric hosts cover everything from cryptids to ethnic warfare and the 'historical' Man from Hjelmdall. Encyclopedia calls it the radio counterpart of the Graadian fringe science magazine 'Paradox B'.

See also: [Cryptid](#cryptid), [Fascism](#fascism)

### Ubi Dolorianism

**A homespun, flowery folk version of Dolorian architecture, brought to Insulinde by Ubi herdsmen who came on the first boats.**

Conceptualization pictures an old wooden building on the coast as it once was (white and gold with light red flower motifs) and names the style, and you can ask whether you just made the term up. The young man Noid confirms it: 'folk Dolorianism', an early, mass-produced subset of Dolorian building. In his words, nearly everything between an ancient concrete cathedral and a glass cube counts as Dolorianism.

See also: [Ubi Sunt?](#ubi-sunt), [Dolorian](#dolorian), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Conceptualization](#conceptualization)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Noid believes the Ubis built the old church about 320 years ago as a kind of sarcophagus: a structure meant to surround and contain something strange they had heard about but did not really understand.

</details>

### Un Pays Infernal

**A dense anti-capitalist book of critical theory; the student communist reading group in the apartment building has worked through it.**

The title is French for 'An Infernal Country'. The reading group lists it among their past assignments and credits it to Abelard; another description names the author as Dr. Benoit Mejean, an angry humanities doctor at La Cherte University in Sur-La-Clef, who promises to cover everything wrong with capitalism once and for all.

The book jokes that the more communist books you read, the more communist your brain becomes, but in practice it reads as near-impenetrable jargon.

See also: [Communism](#communism)

### White Deora

**A dusty, broken pinball machine at the Whirling-in-Rags, themed on early airships and beautiful, sad, pearl-laden women of the Dolorian age.**

Kim guesses it's about Messina in the Dolorian era and finds history-themed pinball inane. Encyclopedia explains that Deora was one of the three Crown Cities of the Dolorian era on the Mundi isola, alongside Rheasilvia and Advesperascit. None of these machines work; the only working one is the Royalist Pinball in the main hall.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Dolorian](#dolorian), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Wholeness, Unity, Balance

**A self-help health book by Matthias W. Dundas on the paranormal shelf of Plaisance's bookstore, claiming the mind can cure nearly anything.**

Like many books on that shelf, it gives medical advice to people who can't afford doctors while dismissing real medicine. Its logic: if your mind has to heal you, it was your mind that made you ill. Chapters cover harvesting magnesium from plants, carrying on drinking with a ruined liver, and an old Seraise remedy using duck gall bladder. It is not worth buying.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

### Wirrâl

**A hugely popular fantasy board game of maps, dice and miniatures, with welkin, dweorgr, faerie folk and pygmies; you can buy a module at the bookstore.**

Its box promises an escape from modern life into a pastoral world of magic, monsters and dungeons, free of isolas, nations and technology. Players create heroes from five races (welkin, dweorgr, humans, faerie folk and pygmies), read an intricate rulebook and roll dice. The setting is known for its complicated system of political alignments, though most players just hack through dungeons for loot.

Plaisance sells a Third Edition module at her bookstore. Inside are a map, a booklet, a 24-sided die and a little plastic figurine of a communard, which clearly doesn't belong to the game.

*Real-world inspiration:* Dungeons & Dragons and similar Tolkien-style fantasy games (the welkin and dweorgr are its elves and dwarves).

See also: [Wirrâl Untethered](#wirrâl-untethered), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Isola](#isola)

### Wirrâl Untethered

**An ambitious radio game set in a version of the Wirrâl world, developed by Fortress Accident, a failed studio in the Doomed Commercial Area.**

The idea was an automated role-playing game run on radiocomputers: players anywhere could join through call-in stations with two-way radios, while a game master frequency coordinated the stories. Its world was a modified Wirrâl with heat death thrown in.

The studio had 18 staff and tried to make a 4,000,000 reál game with 400,000 in the bank. The Novelty Dicemaker sells a special die for it, decorated with motifs of ice, death and loss.

See also: [Wirrâl](#wirrâl), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Radiocomputer](#radiocomputer)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The game was lost: an unexplained numeric anomaly struck just as the project was being compiled, and it came back completely blank.

</details>

## Items

### A Brief Look at Infra-Materialism

**A pocket guide to infra-materialism, a mystical branch of communist theory; the student communists give it to you as your first reading assignment.**

Infra-materialism grew out of the ideas of Ignus Nilsen, one of Kras Mazov's closest associates. It claims that thoughts radiate out of the brain as politically charged energy called 'plasm', and that a community with enough revolutionary fervour could start to change physical reality: first boosting crop yields (and beards), and at the highest level suspending the laws of physics.

The guide admits that plasm has never been observed and that critics on all sides call it nonsense. Reading it lets you keep up with the reading group in the apartment building.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov)

### A Folded Library Card

**A rain-soaked Jamrock library card found on a dead man on the Martinaise boardwalk; it is your first lead to who he was.**

The card was issued by the Central Jamrock Public Library to someone named Billie Méjean, and the back lists recently borrowed books, mostly science fiction and thrillers. You can call the library from the radio in Kim's motor carriage to learn more.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Kineema](#kineema), [Jamrock](#jamrock)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Billie Méjean is the dead man's wife; he is her husband, Victor Méjean. The library can give you the family's home address in the apartment building by the pier, where the news has to be delivered.

</details>

### A Note from the Fridge

**A note found in a giant ice-bear-shaped fridge in a cellar of the Doomed Commercial Area; it says where a valuable radiocomputer backup was hidden.**

The note is signed by Sulisław and says that an 'off-site copy' on a filament memory (a storage part for a radiocomputer) was moved into a frozen ice cream maker, out of reach of a thieving ginger kid. Kim has a good guess who that kid is.

It helps with the errand Soona the programmer gives you: recovering that copy from her old workspace.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Radiocomputer](#radiocomputer), [Cuno](#cuno)

### Ammonia ampoule

**A small capsule of ammonia to sniff; it helps you keep your stomach down while examining the reeking corpse behind the Whirling-in-Rags.**

When the smell of the hanging body and your hangover make you want to throw up, Kim suggests some ammonia to clear your head. Having it gives you a bonus on the check to hold it in, and lets you retry that check. The gardener in the plaza, who uses ammonium salts herself, can give you one, and the Frittte sells it too.

Soona the programmer notes that, in a pinch, ammonia also works for cleaning oily radio antenna connections.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Frittte](#frittte), [White check](#white-check)

### Amphibian sports visor

**A sporty visor sold by Siileng from his box of sunglasses.**

See also: [Siileng](#siileng)

### Antique rifle

**An old Belle-Magrave rifle from the Revolution that you can find in a hidden weapons cache. It's beautiful, but it no longer fires.**

Kim identifies it as a Revolution-era Belle-Magrave: a Revacholian-made, breech-loading rifle in 4.46 calibre, known above all for its reliability. He admires it even though it has clearly been out of order for years. It lies with other, more damaged weapons in a cache hidden in a hole in the wall of the Doomed Commercial Area.

You can show it to people around Martinaise, such as René Arnoux, Titus Hardie or Steban the student communist, to hear what they make of it.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Its 4.46 mm calibre matches the bullet that killed the Hanged Man, which points you toward an old military rifle as the murder weapon (though not necessarily a Belle-Magrave).

</details>

### Apricot Chewing Gum Wrapper

**A crinkled 'Tutti Frutti' gum wrapper smelling of apricot and cinnamon, found in a pocket; it stirs a painful, half-buried memory.**

You find it in the pocket of a pair of work jeans once you put them on. Encyclopedia notes that apricot gum is made in Sur-la-Clef from Siigayan apricots, but your skills care more about the smell, which feels terribly familiar. Inland Empire begs you to throw it away.

See also: [Siigay](#siigay), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Limbic System](#limbic-system)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The scent is tied to Harry's memories of the woman who left him; the Ledger of Failure smells of the same gum, and your mind calls her 'The Apricot Chewing Gum Scented One'.

</details>

### Armistice

*Also: Kiejl A9, Kiejl A9/90, Armistice A9, A9 Armistice*

**Kim Kitsuragi's sidearm: a cheap, mass-produced Kiejl A9 muzzleloader, one of the most common guns in the world. He lends it to you only briefly.**

Encyclopedia describes the Kiejl A9/90 Armistice as a frugal, mass-produced single-shot muzzleloader. Like other muzzleloaders it has to be reloaded after every shot or two, but Kim insists it is precise enough at close range. Your hands seem to remember it, as if it was once your own weapon of choice.

Kim may hand it to you at a few tense moments, at the hanging tree or in the Whirling with the Hardie Boys, and he always takes it straight back.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Bullet](#bullet)

### Astra

*Also: Astra Whites, Astra Menthol, Red Astra*

**A legendary cigarette brand from Graad; with the local Drouin, it's what most Revacholians smoke today. The Frittte sells it.**

Variants you hear about include Astra Whites, Astra Menthol (Klaasje's brand) and Red Astra, a high-tar black-market version. You can buy a pack at the Frittte or from Rosemary in the Fishing Village, and the racist lorry driver in the traffic jam gives you one if you play along with him. Cuno will happily take a pack too.

Cigarettes raise your Intellect skills at the cost of Health, and lighting up can restart your old smoking habit.

See also: [Frittte](#frittte), [Tioumoutiri](#tioumoutiri), [Cuno](#cuno), [Intellect](#intellect)

### BADGE: LTN-2JFR DU BOIS

*Also: LTN-2JFR DU BOIS*

**Your RCM police badge, missing when the game begins; once found, it confirms your name, rank and precinct.**

The badge is a bluish card sealed in plastic, with an old photo of you, a watermark of Revachol West's street grid and a serial number. It names you as Harrier Du Bois of Precinct 41 in Jamrock, with the rank LTN-2JFR (lieutenant double-yefreitor), and was issued about four months ago.

Losing it is serious: one of your first tasks is to report it missing to your station.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Yefreitor](#yefreitor), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

You can get it back very late in the case, out by the fishing village, in the company of officers from Precinct 41.

</details>

### Blue medicinal spirit

*Also: medicinal spirits*

**A bottle of 98.7% pure blue alcohol that Rosemary sells in the Fishing Village for 300 réal. Your necktie insists you need it.**

Rosemary, the drunk shopkeeper of the Union of Moribund Alcoholics, says he got it from a doctor's office and calls it magic. The Horrific Necktie gives you a task built around it: buy the spirits and hold on to them until the time is right.

See also: [Horrific Necktie](#horrific-necktie), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

After Ruby's scene, the necktie leads a 'dark celebration' that turns the bottle into a spirit bomb. Keep it in hand during the violent standoff in the plaza: you can light it and throw it.

</details>

### Blue oblong pen

**A plain blue pen that Kim Kitsuragi gives you when you need to write something, since you clearly don't own one.**

Kim hands it over when you need to write in your ledger or deal with paperwork, either kindly (he says he has another) or, if you push him, with open resentment. It's handy whenever someone has to sign something, such as the documents from Evrart Claire that need signatures in the Fishing Village, and you can lend it out or scribble comments in a magazine with it.

The only other pen around is a green one decorated with the 'kind green ape' cryptid.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire)

### Boots with Rotting Flesh

**The hanged man's armoured boots, pried off the corpse with bits of him still stuck inside; cleaned up, they would make fine footwear.**

They are high-tech ceramic sabatons marked 'Fairweather T-500 / VE', covered in graceful whorls that hide a serial number. They need a thorough cleaning before you can wear them, and the industrial stove in the Whirling-in-Rags kitchen is one way to do it.

The serial number can be run through Alice, the radio officer at Precinct 57.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Alice](#alice), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

### Box with locusts

**A fresh batch of live locusts that Morell the cryptozoologist gives you to restock the empty bait trap in the reeds.**

Morell and his wife Lena hunt the Insulindian Phasmid with funnel traps baited with locusts, hoping the creature will crawl in to feed and get stuck. When one trap turns up empty, you can promise to refill it, and Morell hands you the locusts to release into it.

See also: [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid), [Lena](#lena), [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

No phasmid ate the missing locusts: Cuno took them for his 'Locust City'.

</details>

### Bullet

**A single 9 mm bullet for a muzzleloader pistol. With your own gun and ammunition missing, it may be the only round you get.**

It fits any 9 mm muzzleloader, the kind of sidearm RCM officers carry, such as the Villiers pistol you are supposed to own. You can find it hidden under a loose floorboard in the shack in the Fishing Village.

See also: [Armistice](#armistice), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It suggests Ruby has been secretly staying in the shack, and Kim says it's enough to confront the washerwoman with. Loaded into a gun, it gives you a shot during the violent standoff in the plaza.

</details>

### Carabineer uniform

*Also: Royal Carabineer uniform*

**A very colourful old royalist uniform of the Royal Carabineers.**

See also: [Royal Carabineers](#royal-carabineers), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It belongs to René Arnoux, and you can find it in the night watchman's booth in the cargo area.

</details>

### Chaincutters

*Also: chain-cutters, chaincutter*

**Rubber-handled cutters from the toolbox in Kim's motor carriage. You need them to cut through the hanged man's steel-reinforced belt.**

Kim keeps a pair of RCM-issue chaincutters in the toolbox of his Kineema and lets you use it. Besides freeing the body for the autopsy, they can snip chains and corroded padlocks on doors around Martinaise, though they're blunter than they look. You can even use them on the leaky faucet in the Whirling's bathroom.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

### Cindy's brush

**The paintbrush of Cindy the SKULL, a young graffiti artist by the pier. Get it from her and you can paint your own message on the wall.**

Cindy paints her big red mural with heavy fuel oil rather than paint. With the right approach she'll drop her brush for you, especially once she's out of fuel oil. Pair it with the red-dyed heavy fuel oil in the fuel canister from Kim's Kineema to add your own contribution to the wall, which counts toward Art Cop.

See also: [Cindy's painting](#cindys-painting), [Kineema](#kineema), [Copotype](#copotype)

### Cindy's painting

**A painting by Cindy the SKULL that you can buy from her and resell at a profit, as part of your ultraliberal dream of getting rich.**

Cindy normally refuses to sell her art, since street art is meant for everyone, but she keeps a small painting tucked in her coat and will part with it for the right price. Savoir Faire immediately starts looking for a buyer. The Mega Rich Light-Bending Guy in the container yard is interested if you sell him a good story with it, and even a name.

Completing the sale moves your get-rich plans forward; he suggests your next step should be a Personal Brand Strategist.

See also: [Cindy's brush](#cindys-brush), [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism)

### Commemorative pin

*Also: Lena's pin*

**A tarnished gold pin from the 'Saint-Batiste Summer '31 Dinghy Races' that kind old Lena gives you to pawn when you ask her for money.**

It shows three sailboats with the sun behind them. Bird's Nest Roy at the pawnshop will pay 3.20 réal for it (a little over its value), and you can ask him to hold on to it for a while. Lena would clearly rather keep it, so you can give it back, or buy it back from Roy and return it, which is the honourable choice.

See also: [Lena](#lena), [reál](#reál)

### Commodore Red

**A cheap red wine sold in big green glass bottles; it seems to have been a favourite of yours during the binge that wiped your memory.**

It comes in 1.5 litre bottles. Garte, the Whirling's manager, may hand you the rest of his bottle once his love troubles with Sylvie are settled. Like all alcohol it boosts your Physique skills at the cost of Morale, and opening a bottle can restart your old drinking habit. Idiot Doom Spiral in the Fishing Village will trade stories for a bottle.

See also: [Amnesia](#amnesia), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry), [Frittte](#frittte), [Garte](#garte), [Morale](#morale), [Physique](#physique)

### Cursed die

*Also: cursed dice*

**A tiny black sphere marked with six fatalistic phrases, which the Novelty Dicemaker makes for you if you ask for a 'cursed' die.**

Order it from Neha, the Novelty Dicemaker in the Doomed Commercial Area, by asking for a die as cursed as the area (or as your life). It costs seven réal and takes eight hours. The sphere pretends to be a six-sided die; its faces read 'God is indifferent', 'Take all', 'Lose all', '50/50', 'Nothing happens' and 'pale'. Neha says the design comes from Iilmaraa, where such dice were used to tell fortunes, and insists it isn't really cursed.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Wirrâl Untethered die](#wirrâl-untethered-die), [Pale](#pale)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Neha has weighted it, so every roll lands on 'God is indifferent'. That, she says, is the curse.

</details>

### Damaged Ledger

*Also: Ledger of Failure and Hatred, Ledger of Oblivion, Oblivion Ledger, Ledger of (Oblivion Induced) Mental Health*

**Your official RCM ledger: a blue clipboard of soggy forms and case notes, fished out of a trash container. Reading it turns up pieces of your past.**

It holds old forms, summonses, notes on past cases and a few personal keepsakes, all damp and reeking of rubbish. Early on you use it to give the case an official name.

Later it turns into a special tool you can hold in your hand for skill bonuses.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

A card from a woman in your past is tucked inside. If you read it you black out, and Kim hands the ledger back as the Ledger of Failure and Hatred; if you let the wind take the card, it becomes the Ledger of Oblivion instead.

</details>

### Deluxe Topping Pie

**A fancy vegan topping pie (pizza) with a wheat-free crust, given to you by Gary, a topping-pie courier; eating it restores some Health.**

Topping pie is the local name for a pizza-style pie. Gary, Morell's grumpy companion out by the reeds, admits he works as a 'special' topping pie delivery courier and offers you Revachol's best. This deluxe one has meatless bacon, avocado, pineapple, fig and basil on a wheat-free crust.

Authority considers it a bribe from a sleazy man. You can eat it, throw it on the ground, or try to share it with Kim, who is not keen.

See also: [Health](#health), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Dick Mullen and the Mistaken Identity

*Also: Mistaken Identity*

**A brittle Dick Mullen paperback from the local bookstore, in which Mullen is accused of murder; the last part of the book is missing.**

You can buy it from Plaisance's crime shelf, which is crammed with novels about the Vespertine detective Dick Mullen. In this one, Mullen drives his old friend Charlie Spillane to a coastal town, passes out drunk at home, and is later charged with Spillane's murder. Released for three days to find the real killer, he meets the mysterious Deanna Deneuve and stumbles onto an amphetamine smuggling ring.

Kim says the books are about dames and gunplay rather than real police work, and Esprit de Corps is offended by the police procedure.

See also: [Annette](#annette), [Dick Mullen](#dick-mullen)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

There is no other copy to be found. In the end you have to decide for yourself who the killer was, and your skills happily go along with whatever you choose.

</details>

### Dick Mullen hat

*Also: Mullen hat*

**A hat like the one fictional detective Dick Mullen wears, given to you by Annette for helping out.**

See also: [Annette](#annette)

### Dockworker ID card

*Also: shift card*

**A plastic shift card you can pickpocket from a dockworker snoring in the Whirling-in-Rags. It belongs to a man named John.**

Slipping it off him takes some Interfacing. You can then flash it at the harbour gates and claim to be a fellow worker, which fools no one, since the photo isn't you. Call Me Mañana, the dockworker at the gates, knows John and offers to get the card back to him; handing it over is the honourable choice.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Union](#union), [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Dried Wildflower

*Also: dried May bells, dried May bell*

**A few crumbling white petals you can catch on the Whirling-in-Rags roof, outside Klaasje's window; a small, odd clue.**

A handful of dried white wildflowers lies on the roof outside Klaasje's top-floor window. With quick hands you can save one before the wind blows the rest away; it then sits in your inventory as the Dried Wildflower.

Kim identifies it as a May bell (the Insulindian Lily), a spring flower that should not be blooming yet and looks deliberately dried. He and your skills suggest showing it to someone who remembers the Revolution, such as the old veteran René on the plaza.

See also: [May bell](#may-bell), [Klaasje](#klaasje), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The flowers were left on purpose by Iosef Lilianovich Dros, the old deserter on the Sea Fort island, where May bells grow wild. Linking the flower to him helps you reach the end of the case.

</details>

### Fairweather T-500

*Also: T-500, T500*

**A futuristic ceramic armour set whose pieces (helmet, plates, gauntlets, boots) turn up around Martinaise.**

Gathering the whole set is a side activity, and people comment when you wear it.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The armour originally belonged to the hanged man, who was stripped of it.

</details>

### Filthy Jacket

**A jacket so caked in sea muck and seagull droppings that it crunches; you can find it hanging on a railing by the boardwalk.**

The jacket seems to have spent time in the sea, been dragged along the beach and then baked in the sun on a railing. Examining it too closely only gets the filth on your hands.

It belongs to Idiot Doom Spiral in the Fishing Village, who has lost it. You can give it back as it is, or first have it cleaned by the washerwoman in the village.

See also: [Idiot Doom Spiral](#idiot-doom-spiral), [FALN](#faln), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Once washed, it turns out to be a perfectly good FALN jacket you can wear.

</details>

### Flashlight

**A sturdy blue police-issue flashlight kept in Kim's motor carriage; you need it in dark places.**

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Frittte raincoat

*Also: plastic raincoat*

**A cheap plastic raincoat from the Frittte store's knick-knacks stand. You can buy it, or try stealing it.**

See also: [Frittte](#frittte)

### From A to Zrieek! A Guide To a Well-Behaved Cockatoo

*Also: From A to Zrieek, From A to Zrieek!, guide to cockatoos*

**A colourful guide to pet cockatoos sold in the Martinaise bookstore; reading it lets you decide which kind of cockatoo you are.**

Plaisance sells it at Crime, Romance, and Biographies of Famous People, or you can try to steal it. The book stresses that every cockatoo species is needy, loud and full of behavioural problems.

The idea of a heraldic bird comes up while talking to the Working Class Woman in the plaza. Reading about the funeral cockatoo, the Major Majestic and the common grey 'bang-bang' lets you decide which one you are (or that you're a 'fuckatoo'), nudging your copotype toward Apocalypse, Superstar, Boring or Sorry Cop.

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

### Fuck the World jacket

**A jacket you can get from the two kids hanging around the plaza; wearing it unlocks some very crude lines.**

### Fuel canister

*Also: fuel canisters*

**A can of fuel; one is RCM issue, another can be found elsewhere. Fuel is needed to power a generator later on.**

The RCM canister can also be used, with a brush, to paint a message on a wall.

### Gardening gloves

*Also: garden gloves*

**Rubber gloves you can borrow from the Gardener on the plaza, handy when you need to examine a body.**

See also: [The Gardener](#the-gardener)

### Gaston's Sandwich

**Gaston Martin's enormous ham sandwich, loaded with mayo, roasted onions and tomatoes; you can coax it off him on the plaza.**

Gaston keeps his lunch with him while playing pétanque with René. With the right words you can persuade him to hand it over.

Eating it restores a little health, and it is famously delicious. You can also share it with Kim or offer it to René.

See also: [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Health](#health)

### Hanged man's boots

**The boots you can pry off the corpse in the yard. They come off in a truly disgusting way.**

Boiling them on the Whirling's stove cleans them up.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Fairweather T-500](#fairweather-t-500)

### Hjelmdallermann: the Man from Hjelmdall

**A tattered Man from Hjelmdall paperback sold at the bookstore, advertised as a good introduction to the series.**

The cover shows the hero standing on a pile of corpses with his twin greatswords, Sturm and Drang. Inside, he and his blood-brother Tyrbald fight off an undead army, then take on a quest for the southern king Lord Wrôthgär. It ends on a cliffhanger urging you to buy the next book, 'Hjelmdallermann at the Gates of Tomorrow'.

Cuno, the foul-mouthed kid in the yard, will take a Hjelmdall book off your hands, which can help you make peace with him after a failed attempt to hit him.

See also: [Cuno](#cuno), [Man from Hjelmdall](#man-from-hjelmdall), [Man from Hjelmdall and the Devil Woman](#man-from-hjelmdall-and-the-devil-woman), [Plaisance](#plaisance)

### Horrific Necktie

*Also: necktie*

**A hideous tie you can wear. It talks to you (through Inland Empire) and encourages bad decisions.**

You find it caught on the ceiling fan in your room at the very start. Once Inland Empire has brought it to life and you put it on, it becomes a voice of its own: a party animal that cheers on your worst impulses, especially drinking, smoking and drugs.

See also: [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry)

### Hypnogamma

**A painkiller you can find in the night watchman's booth.**

### La Fumée

*Also: La Fumée, Vol. 1 No. 4*

**A small Martinaise quarterly of radical Mazovian (communist) thought; Kim says the copy you can read was lying around the Union's offices.**

Its name comes from Kras Mazov's phrase 'Du cristal à la fumée', describing how the rigid structures of capitalist ideology turn to smoke under communism. The issue you find has a satirical cover of the late King Frissel wearing white antlers (a communist symbol) and sections on international news, culture ('Kunst und Kultur') and local concerns. It carries no advertising, and Kim suspects the dockworkers' Union bankrolls it.

One essay, a critique of the TipTop Tournée motor carriage race signed 'Nasteb' and 'Exilus', turns out to be the work of the student communists Steban and Ulixes. You can scribble your own comments in it with a pen and show them.

See also: [Communism](#communism), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov), [TipTop Tournée](#tiptop-tournée), [Union](#union)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The essay's two bylines are pen names of the student communists you can meet in the apartment building, and you can show or give them the magazine.

</details>

### Man from Hjelmdall and the Devil Woman

*Also: Devil Woman*

**A well-thumbed Man from Hjelmdall novel from the bookstore, in which the hero is captured by a wicked queen in a jungle citadel.**

The muscle-bound hero crash-lands in a jungle, is captured by female warriors and taken to Cloud City, where the wicked Queen Lydiaana tries to seduce him into leading her armies. You can buy it from Plaisance, the bookseller, and read it from your inventory.

Plaisance calls the series violent and rather immoral, but admits it sells very well. Joyce Messier jokingly borrows the title when talking about women.

See also: [Cuno](#cuno), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Hjelmdallermann: the Man from Hjelmdall](#hjelmdallermann-the-man-from-hjelmdall), [Man from Hjelmdall](#man-from-hjelmdall), [Plaisance](#plaisance)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Cuno will accept this book as a peace offering if you need to make up with him.

</details>

### Medicinal Purposes of the Pale

*Also: Medicinal Purposes*

**An esoteric book of pale-based herbal medicine from the bookstore's paranormal shelf; Encyclopedia calls it quackery.**

The heavy tome claims to collect the wisdom of Seolite healers, Mesque mystics and Iilmaraan folk doctors. It recommends strolls and meditation in the pale, swatting yourself with birch twigs from the 'near-pale', and drinking spirits aged in jars buried just inside the pale. Encyclopedia dismisses it all as quackery.

Its pharmacopoeia lets you combine ingredients such as ginger, mint, birch bark, aloe and whiskey into teas, tinctures and poultices. None of them cures your hangover, but one relaxing tea restores a point of Morale.

See also: [Morale](#morale), [Pale](#pale), [Pale-aged vodka](#pale-aged-vodka), [Plaisance](#plaisance)

### Pale-aged vodka

*Also: pale-aged liquor*

**Rare, very strong and very expensive vodka that has been stored in the pale for years. The Frittte keeps it behind the counter.**

The Frittte clerk explains that it is aged in the pale for a couple of years, which makes it pricey and potent. The book Medicinal Purposes of the Pale recommends burying jars of spirits just inside the pale for 30 to 60 days, depending on the strength you want. Electrochemistry treats a bottle as a gift from the gods, and Idiot Doom Spiral in the Fishing Village will take one in exchange for stories.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Frittte](#frittte), [Medicinal Purposes of the Pale](#medicinal-purposes-of-the-pale)

### Photo of Tattoos

*Also: photo of the tattoos*

**Kim's instant photograph of the hanged man's tattooed chest, taken so you can study the tattoo and show it to people.**

The tattoo is a web of fading blue lines running from the right shoulder to the solar plexus, with a small white star wherever lines cross; the stars cluster around the heart. It could be a star chart, a map or just decoration, and you won't work it out alone, so ask around.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier), [Oranjese Map of the Waterways](#oranjese-map-of-the-waterways)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Joyce Messier recognises it as an Oranjese Map of the Waterways, an old sailor's tattoo that later became a mercenary tradition.

</details>

### Plastic Rain Coat

**A cheap, transparent raincoat with a big 'FRITTTE' slogan on the back, sold at the Frittte convenience store for four réal.**

The coats sit in packets on a low shelf near the counter. You can buy one from the clerk or try to steal one, which the clerk is not happy about if you get caught. Composure may point you to it when it rains.

See also: [Frittte](#frittte), [reál](#reál)

### Police badge

**Your RCM identification badge, which you lost along with your gun during your binge. Finding it is one of your tasks.**

Without it you have trouble proving you're a police officer, and Kim suggests reporting it missing to the station over the radio in his motor carriage. Once you have it back, it shows your full name, your rank (LTN-2JFR, lieutenant double-yefreitor), your precinct, a serial number and an old photo of a younger, winking you.

Looking at it with Kim lets him explain your rank and the RCM's 'décomptage' system of counting ranks down to twos.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Yefreitor](#yefreitor), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Kineema](#kineema)

### Policeman Cloak

*Also: patrol cloak*

**A tarpaulin RCM cloak flapping on a railing high above the harbour's container yard; it is yours, and getting it means a daring jump.**

Esprit de Corps and Perception spot the white RCM rectangle on its back, and Kim agrees it is most likely the service cloak your station issued you. It hangs over a long drop, so you have to leap down from the ledge to grab it (or come at it from below).

The jump also takes you down into the harbour area, a back way in that avoids the strikers at the gates.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Harbour](#harbour), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Savoir Faire](#savoir-faire)

### Porta-reel

*Also: porta-reel boombox*

**The in-world word for a portable tape player. You can buy a gold-and-amber boombox at the pawnshop to play the tapes you find.**

Some evidence comes on tape, such as the 'Great Doorgunner Megamix' Titus Hardie gives you, and you need a player to hear it. Bird's Nest Roy at the pawnshop has a shelf of well-worn boomboxes; the one that catches your eye is a gold-and-amber Harmon Wowshi, and you can haggle over the price.

You can carry it around on your shoulder; when no tape is playing, it tunes back to cheerful radio beats.

See also: [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie)

### Potent Pilsner

**Cheap, mass-produced beer with well over 10% alcohol, sold at the Frittte and by Rosemary in the Fishing Village.**

Electrochemistry calls it piss water but loves it anyway, and Jean Vicquemare mocks how much of it you supposedly drink every hour. Like other alcohol it raises your Physique skills and damages Morale. Idiot Doom Spiral in the Fishing Village will swap stories for one.

See also: [Frittte](#frittte), [Jean Vicquemare](#jean-vicquemare), [Physique](#physique)

### Preptide

**A brand of speed in a nasal-inhaler bottle; there's one in the medicine cabinet on the Whirling's second floor.**

You inhale it through one nostril. It counts as speed for anything that asks for it.

See also: [Speed](#speed), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

### Primer Book

*Also: A Primer for Small Kids*

**A colourful alphabet book for very small children, sold at the bookstore in the Doomed Commercial Area; a task suggests it might help you relearn the basics.**

Its title is 'A Primer for Small Kids', and an anthropomorphic bear guides the reader through it. Each page pairs a letter with one word and a faded picture, from 'Azimuth' and 'Diamat' to 'Warheit' and 'Zenith'. Conceptualization recognises many of the words as IL, the International Language created by scientists from Graad in the Twenties.

The bookseller is baffled that a grown man wants it. Reading it takes a moment and leaves you knowing the alphabet, which you can proudly report to Kim.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Pyrholidon

**A rare synthetic hallucinogen, also used to treat radiation sickness. It boosts your Psyche skills at the cost of Health.**

Electrochemistry calls it the double rainbow of synthetic hallucinogens, a product of the atomic age, and it is hard to find on the street. Bird's Nest Roy at the pawnshop has taken it ever since the People's Pile disaster and can share some with you, and you can also confiscate a phial from the Pigs, an unhinged woman on the boardwalk.

Taking it gives +1 to Empathy, Suggestion, Authority, Inland Empire, Esprit de Corps and Volition, which helps before a White Check, but it damages your Health.

See also: [Psyche](#psyche), [Health](#health), [White check](#white-check)

### RCM cap

**A police cap, part of the RCM uniform, which you can pick up on the boardwalk.**

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Revolutionary figurine

**A little plastic figure of a shouting revolutionary with a musket, part of a collectible set.**

It can be offered as a gift in one of the game's stranger conversations.

See also: [Headless FALN Rider](#headless-faln-rider), [Revolution](#revolution)

### Revolutionary hat

**An outrageously communist hat. Rhetoric hopes nobody sees you in it.**

See also: [Communism](#communism)

### Ruby's Journal

**Ruby's worn brown leather journal, full of coded logistics notes, radio diagrams and frank personal reflections.**

About two thirds of its pages are filled with dated entries. Much of the practical material is in code, the diagrams deal with esoteric radio technology, and the personal passages show someone wrestling with hard decisions and expecting betrayal. Kim thinks a codebreaker at his station could make use of it.

See also: [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

You find it in Ruby's tent in her hiding place. The recent entries show she feared that 'M' (the drug boss known as La Puta Madre) had sent someone to kill her, and she admits that staging a lynching is a crime in itself. Her diagrams relate to a device called the Latitude Compressor.

</details>

### Samaran hat

*Also: black Samaran hat*

**A black hat from Samara. A certain bureaucrat has a story about it.**

See also: [Sunday Friend](#sunday-friend)

### Sixteen Days of Coldest April

**A heavy, grim Graadian novel by Yekartina Dahl, misplaced on the bookstore's romance shelf; you can buy it and try to read it.**

Set during the Yugo-Graad Riots of the Twenties, it follows two young people who fall for each other amid the street violence, told in cold and exhausting detail. The book is so heavy it feels lined with lead. You can push through it (Pain Threshold enjoys the suffering) or decline to subject yourself to Graadian realism.

Steban the student communist may ask what you made of it.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Pain Threshold](#pain-threshold)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The lovers betray each other and are crushed by the state: he ends up a lens-grinder, she spends decades at a fish processing plant, and both die alone with their regrets.

</details>

### Speed

*Also: bottle of speed, vial of speed*

**An amphetamine stimulant sold in small vials; one of the game's four drugs, the one tied to Motorics.**

Cuno keeps a stash and can be talked into splitting some with you. Electrochemistry is always happy to see it.

*Real-world inspiration:* Amphetamine, long nicknamed speed.

See also: [Preptide](#preptide), [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry), [Cuno](#cuno)

### Stock Certificate

**A (photocopied) certificate for shares in Mauer-Koshlai Mercury Group, written in a flamboyant, nearly unreadable script; proof of your new net worth.**

The Mega Rich Light-Bending Guy gives it to you instead of cash when he buys a piece of art from you. Deciphering the curly font takes Perception and Logic; at the top is a sketch of a girl riding an industrial drill above a river of liquid metal.

On the money-minded path you can show it off to various people to prove you are a serious player.

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Logic](#logic), [Perception](#perception)

### Suzerainty: The Board Game

**A 'family' strategy board game from the bookstore in which players are administrators exploiting Revachol's colonies for the old king's glory.**

It costs 12 réal. Players place workers in four vassal lands to gather apricots, marble, sugar and magenta cocaine, fulfil contracts and build monuments back in Revachol for victory points. The cheerful artwork of smiling labourers sits awkwardly with its subject, and Rhetoric suspects a political agenda.

You can open it, read the rules and talk Kim into playing a game with you.

See also: [Suzerain](#suzerain), [Siigay](#siigay), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area)

### The Great Doorgunner Megamix

*Also: Great Doorgunner Megamix, Doorgunner Megamix*

**An audio tape Titus Hardie slams on the table, claiming it proves the dead man was a killer; you need a tape player to hear it.**

The Hardie Boys named it themselves and say you will understand why once you listen. Finding a way to play it (a boombox or a porta-reel) becomes a task, and Klaasje has her own view of what is on it.

See also: [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Klaasje](#klaasje)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It holds drunken, static-filled recordings of the hanged mercenary boasting of war atrocities and threatening the 'dancer' upstairs, which is how the Hardies justify the lynching. An experimental-music fan at the pawnshop would love it.

</details>

### The Greatest Innocence

*Also: Greatest Innocence*

**A huge, heavy history book about the innocences, sold at Plaisance's bookstore; finishing it springs a pop quiz on you.**

Written by João Paolo Salomao Lopez de Fuego, it surveys history and religion through the innocences, the rare world-historical figures who have led the known world. Plaisance calls it an important and daring educational work that re-examines the whole innocentic system.

When you finish reading, the book tests you with four questions: who was the first, the strongest, the false and the greatest innocence. Encyclopedia is very eager to help, though it is not always right.

See also: [Dolores Dei](#dolores-dei), [Encyclopedia](#encyclopedia), [Innocence](#innocence), [The Perikarnassian](#the-perikarnassian)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The book's answers: the first innocence was the Perikarnassian, the strongest was Franconegro, the false one was Ernö Pasternak, and the greatest, in the author's view, is Dolores Dei. Questions about Dolores Dei stir up strange, painful feelings in you that have nothing to do with the quiz.

</details>

### Tioumoutiri

**An old-fashioned cigarette brand with paper filters, lots of tar and a sweet smell, smoked mostly by older men.**

Its logo is the silhouette of a boy in a kofia hat picking tobacco. The brand is named after Tioumoutiri, a former Revacholian colony on Iilmaraa that was known for its tobacco exports. Most Revacholians today prefer Astra or the local Drouin, so Tioumoutiri butts stand out.

See also: [Astra](#astra)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Tioumoutiri butts turn up both at Land's End and in a hidden mattress nest in the old sea fort, suggesting the same person visited both places.

</details>

### Transceiver

*Also: Esterhaz EH-4, Model 9*

**A radio transmitter-receiver. You can pull one out of an abandoned lorry, or buy a pricey Kristallsprach Model 9 from Bird's Nest Roy.**

Some people in Martinaise need one badly for a radio project.

See also: [Bird's Nest Roy](#birds-nest-roy), [Noid](#noid)

### Vodka-borscht

**A thermal cup of borscht spiked with vodka, ladled out by Gorący Kubek, the cook at the Whirling-in-Rags. It counts as alcohol.**

Gorący Kubek doesn't speak your language, but he happily shares his brew, and if you egg him on he pours half a bottle of vodka into the pot. Harry describes it as the special strike brew the Union uses. Like other alcohol it boosts your Physique skills while hurting Morale.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags), [Union](#union), [Physique](#physique)

### White Envelope

**An envelope of legal papers from Evrart Claire for a youth centre in the Fishing Village; it needs signatures from two residents.**

Inside are the construction documents: a building period of 12 to 40 months and a zoning plan for a three-storey youth centre jutting into the sea and covering most of the village's street and square. Evrart wants it signed by Isobel Sadie (the Washerwoman) and Lilienne Carter, the net picker, and then mailed back to him.

See also: [Evrart Claire](#evrart-claire), [Washerwoman](#washerwoman), [Fishing village](#fishing-village), [Union](#union)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Both women are wary of Union paperwork. You can also try forging the signatures or getting the village drunks to sign, but Logic points out that the drunks' signatures are worthless.

</details>

### Wirrâl Untethered die

**A custom role-playing die you can order from the Novelty Dicemaker: alligator jawbone cast in black resin, themed on ice, death and loss.**

Neha, the Novelty Dicemaker in the Doomed Commercial Area, makes a die for whatever role-playing system you name. Ask for one for Wirrâl Untethered (the bleak setting of an unfinished radio game by the studio Fortress Accident) and she designs a variation of the standard Wirrâl die that swaps its plant motifs for ice and death. It costs seven réal and is ready eight hours after you order it.

It feels icy to hold; through the dark resin you can see a nugget of bone from an alligator's jaw.

See also: [Doomed Commercial Area](#doomed-commercial-area), [Cursed die](#cursed-die), [reál](#reál)

### Yellow Man Mug

**A broken mug with a racist caricature of 'the yellow man' frolicking in saffron, which you can dig out of the trash container in the Whirling's yard.**

It was thrown away in the same container as some clothes worth investigating, so it works as a clue: you can show it to people around Martinaise and see who reacts. Trying to have deep thoughts about it mostly gets you told to get back to your case.

See also: [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It matches the collection of colonial mugs in the apartment of Gary, the Cryptofascist, pointing to him as the one who has been dumping things in the Whirling's trash.

</details>

## Slang & Jargon

### aerograffito

**A message painted on rooftops or the ground in letters big enough to be read from the Coalition aerostatics that watch the city from above.**

Kim explains the word when you find a rooftop telling the airships to GO HOME, and again at a giant message on the plaza; he notes that in Revachol West someone is always whispering about Le Retour, 'the Return'.

See also: [Aerostatic](#aerostatic), [Coalition](#coalition), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Al Gul

**Measurehead's grand name for alcohol, which he calls an ancient Iilmaraan poison; you can pick up the phrase and use it yourself.**

To Measurehead, Al Gul is a parasitic fungus from Iilmaraa that has colonised your race: a trick played by the 'desert pygmees' in revenge for being robbed of their land, and the cause of much of your decline. It is race-theory nonsense, but the name sticks. Your detective can claim to have danced with Al Gul for a thousand nights, and Tiago in the old church uses the word too.

*Real-world inspiration:* A pun on 'alcohol' (a word of Arabic origin) and 'ghoul' (al-ghul in Arabic).

See also: [Electrochemistry](#electrochemistry), [Measurehead](#measurehead), [Tiago](#tiago), [Yeesut](#yeesut)

### antecentennial

**From before the turn of the century, i.e. the time of the monarchy. The communist uprising is called the Antecentennial Revolution.**

'Antecentennial nostalgia' is the still-common longing for the days before the turn of the century, kings and all, some fifty years on; you see it in royalist imagery on everything from pinball machines to cigarettes. The Antecentennial Revolution, sparked by the theories of Kras Mazov, ended that world.

See also: [Revolution](#revolution), [Royalists](#royalists), [Kras Mazov](#kras-mazov), [Commune](#commune)

### Cobo

*Also: Il Cobo, Il Cobo di Coppodocia, Cobo mi Lobo, Coppodocia*

**The nickname the hanged man's imagined voice gives you during the autopsy, as in 'Il Cobo di Coppodocia'.**

As you examine the body, your mind lends the corpse a chatty, mocking voice full of fake Messinian-sounding nicknames for you: Cobo, Il Cobo di Coppodocia, Cobo mi Lobo, and endless variations on 'coppo'. The voice itself admits it is a mish-mash of how you imagine people from Messina talk, which says something about your assumptions on where the dead man came from.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Inland Empire](#inland-empire), [Coppo](#coppo)

### Cop Off

**Kim's scornful name for the turf rivalry between Precincts 41 and 57 over who gets to solve the Martinaise hanging.**

The dispatch desk told both stations about the hanging, and the 57th treated it as a contest: time to 'settle it' with a Cop Off. The precincts cannot agree whether Martinaise belongs to Jamrock or to the Industrial Harbour, and Kim thinks this street-gang squabbling over territory is why the Union has been able to make a mockery of law enforcement in the district. He calls the whole thing a pissing competition he wants no part of.

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Precinct 57](#precinct-57), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Martinaise](#martinaise), [Union](#union)

### Coppo

*Also: coppo loco, copporooni, coppodoppo, Coppolini, Coppobollo, Coppolangelo*

**Mocking slang for a cop, used by the Hardie Boys; 'coppo loco' means crazy cop.**

Titus Hardie calls you 'coppo' constantly, and Alain dubs you 'coppo loco' after your antics. The hanged man's imagined voice during the autopsy plays with endless variations on it, and even your own Pain Threshold uses it.

See also: [Pig](#pig), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie), [Cobo](#cobo)

### Décomptage

**The RCM's chain of command, meaning 'counting down to twos': each lieutenant is responsible for two sergeants and eight officers.**

Kim explains it when Harry wonders about his odd rank: the rank above you in your precinct's décomptage might already be taken, or promoted officers sometimes choose not to replace their superiors.

Steban, the student communist, traces this dual leadership back to the Revolutionary Lovers of the Commune era, calls it Revachol's great contribution to revolutionary thought, and believes it survives in the RCM.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Yefreitor](#yefreitor), [Commune](#commune), [Precinct](#precinct)

### Firewalker

*Also: Officer Firewalker*

**A grand call sign your detective can give himself on the radio and elsewhere ('I walk in the flames'); Kim is not impressed.**

You can introduce yourself as Firewalker when calling dispatch from the radio in Kim's motor carriage, or announce it to Gaston Martin. In the Remote Viewer thought, Inland Empire welcomes 'Firewalker' into the RVD (the Remote Viewers Division). Precinct 41 plays along, with plenty of mockery, and Kim simply tells you that you are not a firewalker.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Kineema](#kineema), [Icebreaker](#icebreaker)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

If you climb the horseback monument to radio Coalition Warship Archer, the warship addresses you as Firewalker throughout the call.

</details>

### Franconigerian hard-body

*Also: Franconigerian Hardbody, Franconigerian hardbody*

**Harry's flattering name for a heavy build: 'softly round, yet still in shape', like a stout cavalryman of old.**

You can use the phrase on the working-class woman on the plaza when she implies that you, and her husband, are chubby. Mañana also praises the 'most hard-bodied boiadeiros'. A book on the stands outside the bookstore is titled 'Franconigerian Hardbody: Boiadeiro Culture, Use Your Hardbody'.

See also: [boiadeiro](#boiadeiro)

### Gloaming

**Your detective's ominous name for the coming end of the world; in plain English the word means twilight or dusk.**

Talk of the Gloaming is a staple of the apocalyptic version of your detective: he can greet Garte with it, prophesy it to the Hardie Boys or write it in the autopsy notes. Word gets around: Jean Vicquemare hears you've become 'the mouthpiece of the Gloaming', and Kim would very much prefer you didn't mention it. Joyce Messier tells you there will be no gloaming, only a slow, ugly civil war.

See also: [Apocalypse](#apocalypse), [Copotype](#copotype), [Great Bloodletting](#great-bloodletting)

### Great Bloodletting

**Another of your detective's names for the bloody apocalypse he believes is coming; he was preaching about it during his lost binge.**

According to Idiot Doom Spiral, you spent part of your binge telling strangers that all the blood you had seen as a cop was nothing compared to the Great Bloodletting to come. You can bring it up again when you find your ruined ledger, or as tensions in Martinaise rise, to Kim's dismay.

See also: [Apocalypse](#apocalypse), [Copotype](#copotype), [Gloaming](#gloaming)

### Icebreaker

**What your detective can call himself after giving his face a grim, unyielding look in the bathroom mirror; Kim just wants him to stop.**

At the mirror, Conceptualization compares your newly resolute face to an icebreaker ploughing through the ice toward Katla and Vaasa. You can then announce 'I am Icebreaker' to Kim, or to Gaston Martin, who are both baffled. Kim patiently reminds you that your name is Harrier Du Bois.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Firewalker](#firewalker), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

### Johnny Law

**Old slang for the police, or the law in general; Noid uses it, and you can use it about yourself.**

Noid says suspicious people like him do not spill their secrets to Johnny Law. Harry can also refer to himself this way, for example announcing that Johnny Law is about to 'tear it up, sad-style'.

*Real-world inspiration:* American slang 'Johnny Law' for the police.

See also: [Pig](#pig), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Kipt

*Also: kipts*

**A racial slur for people of Semenese descent; Cuno, the racist lorry driver and the mercenaries all use it freely.**

Encyclopedia explains it targets people of South Semenese or Areopagite descent and was once a common first name among the Areopagites of Iilmaraa.

See also: [Cuno](#cuno), [Mercenaries](#mercenaries)

### Kojko

*Also: Kojkos*

**Slang, often derogatory, for a person from Graad; Kim uses it matter-of-factly, while bigots in Martinaise spit it.**

Kim identifies the Whirling's cook as 'from Graad, a Kojko', and Encyclopedia claims Graadians don't mind such national nicknames. The racist lorry driver, Measurehead and a struggling delivery courier use it with contempt, blaming Kojkos for everything from price dumping to stolen jobs.

Visual Calculus notes that in police suspect descriptions, Kojkos count as white.

See also: [Measurehead](#measurehead), [Fascism](#fascism)

### Men of Wö

*Also: Wö-Men, Wömen, wömen*

**Harry's sneering, heavy-metal-umlaut spelling of 'women', coined by his gut while he works out what fascism means to him.**

It appears in the Revacholian Nationhood thought process, where your stomach decides that wömen are the true enemy. Harry can bring the phrase up again with Measurehead.

See also: [Fascism](#fascism), [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Narcomania

*Also: narcomaniac, narcomaniacs, narco mania, narco-mania, narco*

**An old-fashioned, alarmist word for drug abuse. Harry can adopt it and declare a personal holy war on it.**

Andre uses it to describe the old church as a 'derelict hive of narcomania', and Volition warns that he is only pandering to your conservative side. Klaasje mocks the word ('Narco Mania'). Authority, on the other hand, encourages you to become an anti-narcomania zealot, which can give you a Thought.

See also: [Authority](#authority), [Klaasje](#klaasje), [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Thought](#thought)

### Personal Brand Strategist

*Also: Personal Brand Strategists*

**Business jargon for an image consultant who manages a wealthy client's reputation, investments and public profile.**

Once you start making serious money, a very rich investor advises you to hire one: they do everything from spotting investments and analysing design trends to booking tables at exclusive restaurants. They are drawn to money like sharks to blood, but hard to find in Martinaise, so Savoir Faire suggests asking someone with a past in marketing.

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Savoir Faire](#savoir-faire), [Tequila Sunset](#tequila-sunset)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Savoir Faire's candidate is Idiot Doom Spiral, a former ad man, who offers to take the job himself. As your strategist he dreams up a plan to put your name in lights on the horseback monument, which does not turn out as promised.

</details>

### Picket line

*Also: picket, pickets, picketing, picket lines*

**Strikers gathered at a workplace entrance to stop others from going in to work.**

See also: [Strike](#strike), [Scab](#scab)

### Pig

*Also: pigs*

**Slang insult for a police officer. Cuno uses it on you constantly.**

See also: [Cuno](#cuno), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### porno-tuning

*Also: pornotuning*

**Kim's dismissive word for gaudy car modifications: slammed suspension, giant aero kits, neon underglow and the like.**

Suggest 'slamming' his beloved Kineema or bolting on a massive aero kit and Kim shuts it down: it's porno-tuning, and he says no to the porno.

See also: [Kineema](#kineema), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Motor carriage](#motor-carriage)

### Precinct

*Also: precincts*

**A police district and its station. RCM precincts are numbered (Harry's is 41, Kim's is 57).**

See also: [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Precinct 57](#precinct-57), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Scab

*Also: scabs, strikebreaker, strikebreakers, strike-breaker*

**Insult for someone who works while others are on strike, undermining the strike.**

See also: [Strike](#strike), [Union](#union), [Picket line](#picket-line)

### speedfreak

*Also: speedfreaks*

**Slang for an amphetamine user. In Martinaise it mostly means the young dance-music crowd camped by the old church on the coast.**

Cuno points you to the old church as the place 'where the speedfreaks hang'. The group, around would-be leader Andre and the tinkerer Noid, wants to turn the church into a club for their 'anodic dance music'. Kim just calls them delinquents.

*Real-world inspiration:* Real slang: a 'speed freak' is a habitual user of amphetamines ('speed').

See also: [Dolorian Church of Humanity](#dolorian-church-of-humanity), [Speedfreaks FM](#speedfreaks-fm)

### Station Call

*Also: Station Call slip, station calls*

**An RCM summons slip ordering someone to report to the police station later, used instead of arresting them on the spot.**

Kim prefers serving a Station Call to bringing people in directly, because it avoids confusion and overcrowding at the station. Blank Station Call forms are kept in your ledger, and at certain points in the case you can write one for someone.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Precinct](#precinct), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### tare

*Also: tare machine, tare bag, Tare Drunk*

**Empty bottles collected for their deposit; the tare machine at the Frittte pays ten cents a bottle, a classic way to scrape by in Martinaise.**

You need a tare bag to collect bottles from bins and streets (a drunk passed out on the stairs near the waterfront has one). Feed the bottles to the machine in the Frittte to get cash. Occasionally it swallows your bottles without paying, and kicking it upsets the clerk.

Tare collecting is what the poor do here; Gaston says Evrart gave René a job so a decorated veteran wouldn't be seen picking tare.

*Real-world inspiration:* Bottle-deposit return machines; in Estonian, the developers' language, returnable bottles and packaging are called 'taara'.

See also: [Frittte](#frittte), [reál](#reál), [Hobocop](#hobocop), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux)

### Vacholiere

*Also: Vacholieres, Vacholiero, Vacholiers*

**Another word for a Revacholian, often used to mean a true native of the city whatever their background.**

When Kim speaks up as a citizen rather than a cop, Authority calls him Vacholiere, a Revacholian; you can later call him 'a true Vacholiere' to his face. Acele uses it of rich Revacholians, Call Me Mañana proudly calls himself Vacholiero rather than Mesque, and Gary calls the union men honest Vacholieres. Measurehead, of course, files the Vacholiers among his 'unheroic races'.

Inland Empire senses a whole history behind the word, even if the meaning is plain.

See also: [Revachol](#revachol), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Measurehead](#measurehead)

### Weird Cop

**Kim's dry name for your cryptic, insinuating style of questioning. He thinks it only confuses everyone and you should just ask normal questions.**

He coins it at the pétanque game with Gaston, after a bout of strange behaviour that he takes for some experimental interrogation technique. Kim says he knows a Weird Cop when he sees one.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Copotype](#copotype), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

### Yefreitor

*Also: yefreitors, double-yefreitor, lieutenant double-yefreitor, Lieutenant double-yefreitor, lieutenant-yefreitor, LTN-2JFR*

**An RCM rank add-on given to officers who turn down a promotion; Harry, a 'lieutenant double-yefreitor', has declined twice.**

Kim explains that 'yefreitor' is added to your rank when you decline a promotion to a higher rank. Harry turned down promotion to captain twice, so his full rank is lieutenant double-yefreitor, though people also often call him 'lieutenant-yefreitor'.

*Real-world inspiration:* From the Russian 'yefreytor' (from German 'Gefreiter'), a junior military rank similar to lance corporal.

See also: [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia)

### Yellow Man

**A racist caricature. A broken mug in the Whirling's trash shows 'the yellow man' frolicking in saffron, and the phrase comes up again later.**

You can pick the mug out of the trash container in the Whirling-in-Rags yard. Showing it around gets very different reactions: a racist lorry driver finds it hilarious.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Seol](#seol), [Whirling-in-Rags](#whirling-in-rags)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Gary, the cryptofascist on the coast, blurts out 'Yellow Man!' at Kim, who is of Seolite descent, and later apologises. The mug matches the collection of racist 'colonial' mugs in his apartment, suggesting he dumps his trash in the Whirling's container.

</details>

## Vocabulary

### Allumer

**French for 'switch on' or 'light up': a label on one of the dial keys of an old Feld control console.**

Other labels on the same console are French too, such as Radiodiffusé ('broadcast') on its frequency band.

### Amnesia

*Also: amnesiac*

**Loss of memory. Your detective has a severe case of it after his binge.**

### Apocalypse

*Also: apocalyptic*

**The end of the world. Some versions of your detective become obsessed with it ('Apocalypse Cop').**

See also: [Copotype](#copotype), [Pale](#pale)

### Arrêt

**French for 'stop' or 'off': one of two worn buttons on the crane control panel in the container yard, next to Marche ('on').**

The panel controls the big crane above it, which has a shipping container hanging from its hook.

See also: [Harbour](#harbour)

### Cochonnet

**French for the small target ball in pétanque: players try to throw their metal boules as close to it as possible.**

Gaston Martin explains the rules as he and René play in the crater on the plaza: a coin toss decides who throws the cochonnet, the players stand in a rope circle with both feet planted, and the distances to the cochonnet are measured at the end.

See also: [Pétanque](#pétanque), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin), [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux)

### Contact mic

*Also: contact microphone, contact mics, contact microphones*

**A microphone that picks up sound from inside solid things, like wood or ice, instead of from the air; Acele uses one for her field recordings.**

Acele, a young sound experimenter you meet on the frozen bay near the church, explains that her contact mic records sounds from inside things. Harry may mishear the name and start talking about Contact Mike, the boxer, instead.

### Cryptid

*Also: cryptids*

**An animal that some people believe exists but science has not confirmed.**

See also: [Cryptozoology](#cryptozoology), [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid)

### Cryptozoology

*Also: cryptozoologist, cryptozoologists, cryptozoological*

**The study of animals whose existence isn't proven (like Bigfoot or the Loch Ness monster).**

See also: [Cryptid](#cryptid), [Lena](#lena), [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid)

### Déjà vu

*Also: deja vu*

**French for 'already seen': the eerie feeling that you've lived this moment before.**

### Eau-de-Nil

**French for 'water of the Nile': the name of a pale green colour. Inland Empire uses it to describe Joyce Messier's eyes.**

See also: [Joyce Messier](#joyce-messier)

### Ennui

**French: a deep, listless boredom and dissatisfaction with life.**

### Entropy

*Also: entropic*

**Physics: the tendency of everything to fall into disorder. Used poetically for decay and the end of things.**

See also: [Pale](#pale)

### Liebling

**German for 'darling'. You hear it in stray foreign-language radio chatter while trying to raise the Coalition warship Archer.**

The voice belongs to a worried parent arguing with their partner. Roughly translated: 'No, darling! I won't allow it... How can you send our boy to Vredefort on one of those things?' and 'Darling, please... He's our only child. He can go to the academy next year.'

See also: [Luftschiff](#luftschiff), [Coalition](#coalition)

### Lorryman

*Also: lorrymen*

**A lorry (truck) driver; several lorrymen and their lorries are stuck in the traffic jam at the roundabout outside the harbour gates.**

Lorrymen have their own guild, the Lorrymen and Carters Guild. Joyce Messier suggests interviewing the drivers still waiting in the traffic jam, since one of them may be expecting an important shipment. The Paledriver, who drives across the pale, calls herself a camionneur.

See also: [Paledriver](#paledriver), [Harbour](#harbour)

### Luftschiff

**German for 'airship' (an aerostatic in Elysium). It turns up in stray foreign-language radio chatter while you try to contact the Coalition warship Archer.**

Rhetoric struggles with the language. Roughly translated, the voice says that, according to a woman named Marianne, a man called Oskar has not been the same since he came back on an airship from Graad: the psychologists call him perfectly normal, but she feels she has been living with a stranger since his return.

See also: [Liebling](#liebling), [Aerostatic](#aerostatic), [Coalition](#coalition)

### Lynching

*Also: lynch, lynched, lynch mob, lynchings*

**A killing by a mob without trial, often by hanging. The Hanged Man is said to have been lynched.**

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Hardie boys](#hardie-boys)

### Marche

**French for 'on' (literally 'running'): the start button on the old crane control panel at the container yard. Its partner, Arrêt, means 'off'.**

See also: [Harbour](#harbour)

### Memento mori

**Latin for 'remember that you must die': a reminder of death meant to show how vain and passing earthly pursuits are.**

Encyclopedia explains the phrase when Titus Hardie tosses it into conversation, and the rotting hanged man himself boasts of his 'memento mori features'.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Titus Hardie](#titus-hardie)

### Nihilism

*Also: nihilist, nihilists, nihilistic*

**The belief that nothing has meaning or value.**

See also: [Solipsism](#solipsism), [Ennui](#ennui)

### OFCs

*Also: Offshore Financial Centres*

**Short for 'Offshore Financial Centres': a polite name for tax havens, places where the wealthy park money out of reach of taxes.**

The term comes up in the harbour's container yard, where Savoir Faire is convinced that someone nearby has assets stashed away offshore. Encyclopedia adds that the EPIS nations have a framework for dealing with OFCs, but they are still a problem.

See also: [Ultraliberalism](#ultraliberalism), [Savoir Faire](#savoir-faire)

### Oxygen holocaust

**A mass extinction billions of years ago, when early life began producing oxygen and poisoned the organisms that lived without it.**

In real-world science it is better known as the Great Oxygenation Event. In the game it comes up as a comparison for an even bigger catastrophe.

See also: [Pale](#pale), [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid), [Entropy](#entropy)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The Insulindian Phasmid compares what the spreading pale will do to the oxygen holocaust, only much worse.

</details>

### Pétanque

*Also: petanque, boules, boule*

**A French ball game: players toss metal balls to land as close as possible to a small target ball.**

In Martinaise two old men, René and Gaston, play it in a crater near the plaza. Players throw their boules as close as they can to the small target ball, the cochonnet.

See also: [René Arnoux](#rené-arnoux), [Gaston Martin](#gaston-martin)

### Radiodiffusé

**French for 'broadcast': the label on the frequency band of an old, dusty Feld control console.**

The console's other labels are French too: 'Allumer' ('switch on') and 'Urgence -- Ouvert!' ('Emergency -- Open!'). Kim reads them out and guesses the device once controlled the building's electronics.

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

The console stands inside the old sea fort you reach late in the game. Once the generator upstairs has fuel, it can switch on the lights and open a blast door.

</details>

### Rigor mortis

**The stiffening of a body's muscles after death. Useful for estimating time of death.**

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man)

### Solipsism

*Also: solipsist, solipsistic*

**The idea that only your own mind is certain to exist. Also: extreme self-centredness.**

See also: [Nihilism](#nihilism)

### Stevedore

*Also: stevedores, longshoreman, longshoremen*

**A dockworker who loads and unloads ships.**

See also: [Union](#union), [Harbour](#harbour)

### Trauma-and-Stressor Disorder

**The in-world name for lasting psychological damage after trauma, roughly what we call PTSD.**

Joyce Messier says the mercenaries in Martinaise have it after years of combat. Empathy also guesses that Kim avoids Cuno because of his years as a juvenile-crimes officer.

*Real-world inspiration:* Post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), part of the real psychiatric category of trauma- and stressor-related disorders.

See also: [Mercenaries](#mercenaries), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

### Urgence

*Also: Urgence -- Ouvert!*

**French for 'emergency'. 'Urgence -- Ouvert!' ('Emergency -- Open!') is printed on a dial key of an old, dusty Feld control console.**

The console's other labels are also French: 'Allumer' ('switch on') on another key and 'Radiodiffusé' ('broadcast') on the frequency band. Kim reads the labels aloud and guesses the device once controlled the building's electronics.

## Names that are spoilers

<details><summary>Only open this if you've already met these characters in the game.</summary>

### Annouk Meijer-Smit

**A name that comes up late in your dealings with Klaasje, in connection with her passport.**

See also: [Klaasje](#klaasje), [Oranje](#oranje)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Klaasje tells you her real passport is hidden in a plastic buoy in the reeds on the coast, made out in the name Annouk Meijer-Smit. When you find it, the name on it is a different one: she lied to you again, and Kim realises you never learned who she really is.

</details>

### Beautiful Necktie

**What your Horrific Necktie becomes in its final moments, when it speaks to you one last time.**

See also: [Horrific Necktie](#horrific-necktie)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

During the Tribunal, if you light and throw the spirit bomb (a bottle with your tie inside), the burning tie talks to you in a gentler voice. It gives its real name, Joopson AS Men's Fashion model 'Colourful Tie', catalogue no. J327, remembers the day a sad man bought it, and says goodbye.

</details>

### Coalition Shuttle Laurel

**A smaller Coalition aerostatic, one of those hovering over the city, that can be sent to your position during a radio call with the Coalition Warship Archer.**

See also: [Aerostatic](#aerostatic), [Coalition](#coalition), [Committee of Responsibility](#committee-of-responsibility)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It comes on behalf of the Committee of Responsibility to offer you an immediate extraction from Martinaise. Boarding it means abandoning the case, and the game ends.

</details>

### Death Island

**Cuno's grim nickname for the small island with the ruined sea fort, out in the bay off the coast of Martinaise.**

The sea fort was built by King Filippe II, taken over by the Commune, and wrecked during the Landing. Lilienne in the Fishing Village says people died there, and it always seemed like a bad place to her.

See also: [Cuno](#cuno), [Operation Death Blow](#operation-death-blow), [Fishing village](#fishing-village)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Cuno only calls it this if he ends up as your partner for the final stretch of the case, when the investigation finally leads you out to the island.

</details>

### Detached scope

**A lone rifle scope that turns up late in the case, in a very strange place.**

See also: [Insulindian phasmid](#insulindian-phasmid)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It lies in the Insulindian Phasmid's nest, together with a Fairweather T-500 helmet and Klaasje's passport. It seems to be the scope missing from the Deserter's rifle, which he used to watch people across the water.

</details>

### Dora

*Also: Dora Ingerlund, Dora Du Bois*

**A woman from Harry's past whom he cannot stop thinking about.**

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

Dora was Harry's fiancée. Her leaving him years ago is at the root of his collapse, and she haunts his dreams and thoughts throughout the game.

</details>

### Fractured Bullet

**A squashed, military-grade bullet dug out of the hanged man's body during your examination, sealed by Kim in an RCM evidence bag.**

The bullet's yellowish jacket has burst open around a dark core; its base is about 5 mm across and shows faint rifling marks. It is unusual ammunition, and the next step is to find a gun that could have fired it.

See also: [The Hanged Man](#the-hanged-man), [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>

It proves the victim was shot, not simply hanged. Ballistics later match it to the old rifle of Iosef Lilianovich Dros, the Deserter.

</details>

### Harrier Du Bois

*Also: Harry, Harrier, Du Bois, Harry Du Bois, Harry DuBois, Detective Du Bois, Johnny Thundercop, Lieutenant Double-Yefreitor Du Bois, Lieutenant Double-Yefreitor Love, Lieutenant Du Bois, Lieutenant Love, Lieutenant-yefreitor Du Bois, Loonie Roonie, Officer Disco, Officer Harry, Roonie*

**The detective you play: an RCM officer who wakes up with no memory after a catastrophic binge.**

Harry is a lieutenant double-yefreitor of the Revachol Citizens Militia from Precinct 41. At the start of the game he has destroyed his hotel room, lost his gun, his badge and his car, and remembers nothing, not even his own name. He was sent to Martinaise to investigate a hanging.

See also: [Kim Kitsuragi](#kim-kitsuragi), [Revachol Citizens Militia](#revachol-citizens-militia), [Precinct 41](#precinct-41), [Yefreitor](#yefreitor)

<details><summary>Spoiler</summary>
